# RESEARCH — 10 Tool Brands Sold in Canada (Oct 7, 2026)

Compiled from seven research dossiers, captured on 2026-10-06 UTC (the evening of Oct 5 in Toronto). Raw captures are in the session scratchpad (not committed).

# OWNERSHIP AND CORPORATE FACTS: 10 tool brands sold in Canada

Dossier for "We Investigated 10 Tool Brands Sold in Canada (Only 1 Covers Your Cordless Drill for Life)". Uploads Oct 7, 2026.
Compiled 2026-10-06. Every capture is dated 2026-10-06 unless a different date is marked. Raw captures are in `scratchpad/tl_own/`. The CTC AIF is in `scratchpad/ct/aif2025.txt` (captured 2026-10-04, re-read 2026-10-06).

House-rule flags for this dossier:
- All SBD, TTI, Emerson and Lowe's dollar figures are **U.S. dollars, company-wide**. They are not Canadian figures. Say "U.S. dollars" on air.
- **No maker or supplier is named for Mastercraft, MAXIMUM or Powerfist.** None of their own pages names one (see DO-NOT-USE).
- Owning a brand, licensing a name, or being foreign-owned is legal and is not wrongdoing. The script should say so in the ownership beat.

## 2. The ownership twists a viewer will find interesting (all documented)

1. **Two parents account for most of the list.**
   - Stanley Black & Decker's FY2025 10-K names "DEWALT®, CRAFTSMAN®, STANLEY®, BLACK+DECKER® and CUB CADET®" as its brands [S2]. That covers two of the core ten and both SBD alternates.
   - Techtronic Industries (TTI) owns Milwaukee and makes RYOBI under licence [S10, S14].
   - So 4 of the core 10 sit under two companies. RIDGID power tools carry a licensed name (twist 3).
2. **The RYOBI name belongs to a Japanese die-casting company with "no capital relationship" with the company that makes the tools.**
   - Ryobi Limited (est. 1943) now makes "die cast products", builders' hardware and printing equipment [S16b, S16c].
   - On January 10, 2018 it moved its own power-tools business into a new company and transferred 80% of it to Kyocera [S16].
   - Its notice to North American consumers, verbatim: "Ryobi brand power tools and lawn and garden equipment sold in the United States, Canada, Europe, Australia and New Zealand, will remain available in these regions, with Techtronic Industries Co., Ltd., a Hong Kong-based company that has no capital relationship with Ryobi Limited, continuing as the manufacturer and distributor of these products." [S16]
   - The footer of ryobitools.ca, verbatim: "\"RYOBI\" is a trademark of RYOBI Limited and is used by TTI Consumer Power Tools, Inc. pursuant to a license granted by RYOBI Limited." [S12]
3. **The RIDGID name on an orange cordless drill is licensed.**
   - The R86115 drill manual, verbatim: "RIDGID is a registered trademark of RIDGID, Inc., used under license." It names "ONE WORLD TECHNOLOGIES, INC. P.O. Box 1427 Anderson, SC 29622 USA" [S20].
   - Emerson's FY2025 subsidiary list includes "RIDGID, Inc. Delaware" and "Ridgid Online, Inc. Ohio" [S22].
   - Emerson's 10-K lists RIDGID among the brands of its Safety & Productivity segment [S21].
   - TTI's 2025 annual report and its website brand list do **not** mention RIDGID at all [S11, S13]. Do not say "TTI owns RIDGID". The link from One World Technologies to TTI is unverified (U1).
4. **Craftsman: SBD still pays Sears' successor, and Sears' channels kept the name.** From SBD's 10-K filings [S3, S2]:
   - SBD bought the brand from Sears Holdings on March 8, 2017. The "total estimated cash purchase price is $917.4 million on a discounted basis". That was "an initial cash payment of $569.4 million", "a cash payment due in March 2020" (the 10-K's obligations table: "$250 million in March 2020"), plus 2.5% to 3.5% on Craftsman sales in new SBD channels "through March 2032" [S3].
   - SBD "granted a perpetual license to Sears Holdings to continue selling Craftsman®-branded products in Sears Holdings-related channels. The perpetual license will be royalty-free until March 2032 … and 3% thereafter." [S3]
   - FY2025: "the Company paid $ 34.6 million for royalties owed" to "Transform Holdco, LLC, which operates Sears and Kmart retail locations" [S2]. All figures are U.S. dollars.
5. **DEWALT has been part of Black & Decker since 1960.** dewalt.ca: "Black & Decker acquires DEWALT in 1960." [S6]
6. **Milwaukee Tool Canada and RYOBI Canada list the same customer-service number, 1-800-268-4015** [S17, S12b]. Both brands sit under TTI.
7. **A charity foundation holds most of Bosch.** "Ninety-four percent of the share capital of Robert Bosch GmbH is held by Robert Bosch Stiftung GmbH, a company with limited liability and a charitable purpose." [S50]
8. **Canadian Tire is Canadian-controlled by a family.**
   - "Through two privately held companies, Tire 'N' Me Pty. Ltd. and Albikin Management Inc, Martha Billes and Owen Billes beneficially own, control or direct, in aggregate, 2,101,150 Common Shares of CTC." That is 61.4% of the voting Common Shares, as at January 3, 2026 [S1].
   - The CIPO register shows the "MASTERCRAFT MAXIMUM" mark assigned from Canadian Tire Corporation, Limited to "CTC Brand Corp." of Calgary. The change is dated 2026-01-04 [S31]. How CTC Brand Corp. relates to CTC is unverified (U4).
9. **Princess Auto has had one owning family since 1942.** "The original owner sold the company to Harvey Tallman in 1942 for the money he received when he sold his iconic truck." … "Bob's sons Matthew and Marc Tallman taking ownership of the business in 2018." [S32]
10. **Makita is still independent.** It is listed in Japan, has been in Canada since 1973, and is headquartered in Anjo [S40, S43].
11. **HART is being wound down by its owner.** TTI: "the voluntary exit of the HART business (US$156 million in 2025)" [S11].
12. **Kobalt's owner left Canada.** Lowe's "completed the sale of its Canadian retail business" on February 3, 2023. That business ran under the "RONA, Lowe's Canada, Réno-Dépôt, and Dick's Lumber" banners [S61]. Lowe's FY2025 10-K still lists "Kobalt" as a Lowe's private brand [S60].

## 3. Brand-by-brand detail

### 3.1 Mastercraft (Canadian Tire house brand)
- **Owner.** CTC 2025 AIF, dated Feb 18, 2026:
  - "'Owned Brands' refers to brands owned by the Company and sold at the Company's retail banners."
  - "Among the well-known Owned Brands carried at Canadian Tire are Mastercraft, MotoMaster, Noma, Paderno, Sherwood, Simoniz and Yardworks."
  - "The Company's most recognized Owned Brands include Canvas, Dakota WorkPro, Denver Hayes, Diamondback, Mastercraft, MotoMaster, Noma, Outbound, Paderno, Raleigh, Ripzone, Sherwood, WindRiver and Woods, which are primarily sold at the Company's banners." [S1]
- **CTC's own corporate page:** "Built on the foundation of legacy brands such as Mastercraft® and Motomaster®, our Consumer Brands Division is a driving force behind our growth strategy." [S35]
- **Retailer page.** canadiantire.ca meta description: "…tools from Mastercraft, exclusively at Canadian Tire." [S30b]
- **Parent company.**
  - Canadian Tire Corporation, Limited, incorporated in Ontario by letters patent dated December 1, 1927 [S1].
  - Head and registered office: 2180 Yonge Street, Toronto, Ontario, M4S 2B9 [S1].
  - Common and Class A Non-Voting Shares trade on the TSX as "CTC" and "CTC.A" [S1].
  - Founded as a single garage in 1922 by brothers A.J. and J.W. Billes [S1].
- **Sourcing context** (a company filing, not specific to Mastercraft): "approximately 50 percent of Canadian Tire Retail … inventory purchases were sourced directly from vendors outside Canada, primarily from Asia" [S1]. Do not link this sentence to any Mastercraft product.
- **Maker:** not stated on any CTC primary page we read. **Do not name or infer one.**

### 3.2 MAXIMUM (Canadian Tire house brand)
- **Not in the AIF.** It is absent from both Owned Brands lists. A search of the AIF for "Maximum" returned only "maximum capacity" and "maximum of" [S1]. Answer to the task question: MAXIMUM is **not** listed as an owned brand in the 2025 AIF.
- **Retailer page.** canadiantire.ca/en/all-brands/maximum.html meta description: "Shop MAXIMUM Branded Tools to help you tackle any job. Exclusive to Canadian Tire." [S30]
- **Trademark record (CIPO), word mark "MASTERCRAFT MAXIMUM":**
  - Application 1643497, Reg. TMA921795, filed 2013-09-13, registered 2015-11-30, expires 2030-11-30.
  - "Current owner CTC Brand Corp. 110-205 Quarry Park Blvd SE Calgary ALBERTA".
  - "Registered Owner Canadian Tire Corporation, Limited 2180 Yonge Street Toronto".
  - Change in title: "FROM/DE: Canadian Tire Corporation, Limited TO/A: CTC Brand Corp.", dated 2026-01-04 [S31].
  - The standalone "MAXIMUM" mark was not looked up (U5).
- **Script-safe wording:** "Canadian Tire sells MAXIMUM as exclusive to Canadian Tire." Do not call MAXIMUM an "owned brand" citing the AIF.
- **Maker:** not stated. **Do not name or infer one.**

### 3.3 Powerfist (Princess Auto house brand)
- **Trademark.** CIPO, POWER FIST Design:
  - Application 1936688, Reg. TMA1112866, registered 2021-10-29.
  - "Registered Owner Princess Auto Ltd. 475 Panet Road Winnipeg MANITOBA".
  - Goods include "electric hand drills" and "chucks for power drills" [S33].
- **Brand list.** princessauto.com/en/brands lists "Powerfist" [S34].
- **Parent company** (princessauto.com/en/our-story):
  - "Princess Auto is a private, Canadian-owned company employing over 3,600 Team Members, with our Home Office in Winnipeg, Manitoba." … "through over 55 plus stores coast-to-coast" [S32].
  - History: "Princess Auto Wrecking (PAW) began life in 1933. The original owner sold the company to Harvey Tallman in 1942…" … "In 1977, Princess Auto became a retailer, with the first store opening in Edmonton, Alberta." … "Bob's sons Matthew and Marc Tallman taking ownership of the business in 2018." [S32]
  - Answer to the task question: Princess Auto is **private**, based in **Winnipeg**, and run by the **Tallman family** (third generation). The page's own words are "private, Canadian-owned" and "family legacy".
- **Maker:** not stated. **Do not name or infer one.**

### 3.4 CRAFTSMAN (Stanley Black & Decker)
- **Owner.**
  - SBD FY2025 10-K (fiscal year ended Jan 3, 2026; filed 2026-02-24): the Tools & Outdoor segment "carries iconic brands in the industry, including DEWALT®, CRAFTSMAN®, STANLEY®, BLACK+DECKER® and CUB CADET®."
  - "DIY and tradesperson focused products include corded and cordless electric power tools sold primarily under the CRAFTSMAN® and STANLEY® brands" [S2].
- **Acquisition.** SBD FY2017 10-K:
  - "On March 8, 2017, the Company purchased the Craftsman® brand from Sears Holdings Corporation ("Sears Holdings"), which provides the Company with the rights to develop, manufacture and sell Craftsman®-branded products in non-Sears Holdings channels."
  - "The total estimated cash purchase price is $917.4 million on a discounted basis, consisting of an initial cash payment of $569.4 million, which reflects the impact of working capital adjustments, a cash payment due in March 2020 with an estimated present value at acquisition date of $234.0 million, and future payments to Sears Holdings of between 2.5% and 3.5% on sales of Craftsman products in new Stanley Black & Decker channels through March 2032".
  - The 10-K's obligations note: "the Company is obligated to pay $250 million in March 2020."
  - The same 10-K summarises it as "approximately $900 million on a discounted basis" [S3]. All U.S. dollars.
- **Licence back to Sears.** "the Company also granted a perpetual license to Sears Holdings to continue selling Craftsman®-branded products in Sears Holdings-related channels. The perpetual license will be royalty-free until March 2032, which represents an estimated value of approximately $293.0 million, and 3% thereafter." [S3]
- **Still paying in 2025.** "obligation to make future payments to Transform Holdco, LLC, which operates Sears and Kmart retail locations, of between 2.5% and 3.5% on sales of Craftsman products in new Stanley Black & Decker, Inc. channels through March 2032. During the year ended January 3, 2026, the Company paid $34.6 million for royalties owed. The Company will continue making future payments quarterly through the second quarter of 2032." [S2]
- **Brand's own history page** (craftsman.ca): "1927: The CRAFTSMAN® brand makes its debut." … "2017: Stanley Black & Decker purchases CRAFTSMAN®." [S8b]
- **Canada.**
  - craftsman.ca contact: "support.craftsman@sbdinc.com or … 6275 Millcreek Drive, Mississauga, ON L5N 7K6" [S8].
  - Registration "placeOfPurchase" list: "RONA", "KENT", "Amazon" [S8].
  - RONA and RONA+ flyer item "CRAFTSMAN Drill and impact driver combo", valid 2026-10-01 to 2026-10-15 (Flipp, M5B 2H1) [S70]. For prices, see the pricing dossier. This item is availability evidence only.

### 3.5 DEWALT (Stanley Black & Decker)
- **Owner.** SBD [S2]. "Professional products, primarily under the DEWALT® brand, include professional grade corded and cordless electric power tools…" [S2]
- **History** (dewalt.ca and dewalt.com):
  - "1924 (Dec. 29) DEWALT Products Co. is founded."
  - "Black & Decker acquires DEWALT in 1960." [S6]
- **Parent company** (SBD 10-K):
  - "Stanley Black & Decker, Inc. ("the Company") was founded in 1843 by Frederick T. Stanley and incorporated in Connecticut in 1852. In March 2010, the Company completed a merger with The Black & Decker Corporation … incorporated in Maryland in 1910."
  - "principal executive office is located at 1000 Stanley Drive, New Britain, Connecticut"
  - Listed on the NYSE as "SWK"
  - "Approximately 62% of the Company's 2025 revenues were generated in the United States, with the remainder largely from Europe (16%), emerging markets (13%) and Canada (4%)." [S2]
- **Canadian subsidiaries** (SBD Ex-21): "STANLEY BLACK & DECKER CANADA CORPORATION Canada", "STANLEY BLACK & DECKER CANADA HOLDINGS ULC Canada", "Mac Tools Canada Inc.", "MTD Products Limited" [S4].
- **Canada office.** dewalt.ca: "Contact Us at support.dewalt@sbdinc.com or 6275 Millcreek Drive, Mississauga, ON L5N 7K6" [S7].

### 3.6 BLACK+DECKER and STANLEY (SBD alternates)
- Both are SBD brands [S2]. BLACK+DECKER is used "primarily" for "consumer home products such as household power tools, hand-held vacuums, and small appliances" [S2].
- **Canada.** blackanddecker.ca and stanleytools.ca both give "6275 Millcreek Drive, Mississauga, ON L5N 7K6" [S9, S9b].

### 3.7 Milwaukee (TTI)
- **Owner.** TTI 2025 annual report (all versions): "All trademarks listed other than AEG and RYOBI are owned by the Group." [S10, S11] TTI's AR subsidiary table lists "Milwaukee Electric Tool Corporation US US$50,000,000 … 100", described as "Trading and manufacture of power equipment products" [S11].
- **Year acquired.**
  - TTI history page: "2005 Acquired MILWAUKEE, AEG brand license and DreBo brands and businesses for Power Tool and Accessories" [S14].
  - Milwaukee's own page: "So, in 2005, when Techtronic Industries Co. (TTi) acquired Milwaukee®…" and "Since the company began in 1924" [S15].
- **Parent company.**
  - "Techtronic Industries Company Limited … is a public limited company incorporated in Hong Kong and its shares are listed on The Stock Exchange of Hong Kong Limited". Registered office: "29/F, Tower 2, Kowloon Commerce Centre, 51 Kwai Cheong Road, Kwai Chung, New Territories, Hong Kong" [S11].
  - Founded "in 1985 by German entrepreneur Horst Julius Pudwill". "The Pudwill family remains the company's largest shareholder". "constituent stock of the Hang Seng Index" [S10, S11].
  - Listed in Hong Kong in 1990 (stock code 669) [S14].
  - 2025 revenue US$15.3 billion (global, U.S. dollars) [S10].
  - Global R&D for MILWAUKEE is "headquartered in Brookfield, Wisconsin" [S10].
- **Canada.** milwaukeetool.ca/contact: "Milwaukee Tool Canada 3381 Steeles Avenue East. Suite 400 Toronto, ON M2H 3S7" and "Customer Service Canada: 1-800-268-4015" [S17].

### 3.8 RYOBI (licensed name; TTI makes and sells)
- **Name owner.** TTI annual report, press release and website footer: "RYOBI is a registered trademark of Ryobi Limited, and is used under license." [S10, S11, S13]
- **Canadian site.** ryobitools.ca footer: "\"RYOBI\" is a trademark of RYOBI Limited and is used by TTI Consumer Power Tools, Inc. pursuant to a license granted by RYOBI Limited." [S12] "TTI Consumer Power Tools, Inc." appears in TTI's subsidiary table as 100% held, "Trading of power equipment products" [S11].
- **When TTI got it.** TTI history: "2000-2004 Acquired RYOBI brand license and businesses for Power Tool and Outdoor Power Equipment" [S14]. TTI's page does not give an exact year within that range.
- **The name owner.**
  - RYOBI LIMITED, "Established December 16, 1943". Major products: "Die cast products", "Builders' hardware", "Printing equipment" [S16c].
  - Tokyo Stock Exchange, securities code 5851, "listed on October 2, 1961" [S16b].
  - 2018 transfer of its own power-tools business to Kyocera (80%), and the "no capital relationship" statement: see §2 twist 2 [S16].
- **Canada.** ryobitools.ca contact: "Power Tools & Outdoor Products 1-800-268-4015", "Media Inquiries ryobicanada@ttigroupna.com", "Only At The Home Depot" [S12b]. No Canadian street address is shown.

### 3.9 RIDGID (licensed name on power tools)
- **The name on the drill.** R86115 18V brushless hammer drill manual (Home Depot-hosted PDF): "ONE WORLD TECHNOLOGIES, INC. P.O. Box 1427 Anderson, SC 29622 USA 1-866-539-1710 powertools.ridgid.com RIDGID is a registered trademark of RIDGID, Inc., used under license." [S20]
- **Warranty site** (register.ridgidpower.com):
  - Repairs and replacements are handled by "One World Technologies, Inc. ("One World")", at "1428 Pearman Dairy Road Anderson, SC 29625".
  - It also states: "One World Technologies, Inc. and RIDGID®, Inc. are not responsible for direct, indirect, incidental or consequential damages."
  - Footer: "© 2026 RIDGID® ONLINE, Inc." [S23]
- **Trademark holder's parent.**
  - Emerson FY2025 10-K: "Products within our Safety & Productivity segment are marketed under a variety of brands including: Greenlee, Klauke, ProTeam and RIDGID." [S21]
  - Emerson Ex-21 lists "RIDGID, Inc. Delaware", "Ridgid Online, Inc. Ohio", "Ridge Tool Company Ohio" [S22].
  - Emerson Electric Co.: incorporated in Missouri, 8027 Forsyth Blvd, St. Louis; NYSE "EMR" [S21].
  - Emerson's Q3 FY2026 10-Q (June 30, 2026) still reports a Safety & Productivity segment [S24]. In November 2024 Emerson said it was exploring strategic alternatives for that segment (named outlets only, tier b; no transaction found in the 10-Q).
- **TTI link.** TTI's 2025 AR, its press release and its website brand list (MILWAUKEE, RYOBI, AEG, EMPIRE, IMPERIAL BLADES, STILETTO, KANGO, HOOVER, ORECK, VAX, DIRT DEVIL) do **not** mention RIDGID [S11, S13]. The task brief's framing "TTI's licence for RIDGID power tools" is **not** supported by any TTI primary document we found. See U1.
- **Canada.** Home Depot Canada sells RIDGID drills (e.g., R86001K) [S80]. No RIDGID power-tools Canadian office was found (U11).

### 3.10 Makita
- **Owner and parent.** makita.biz corporate profile:
  - "Company Name Makita Corporation Head Office 3-11-8, Sumiyoshi-cho, Anjo, Aichi 446-8502, Japan … Date of Founding March 21, 1915 Date of Incorporation December 10, 1938 … Common Stock Listings Tokyo and Nagoya stock exchanges" [S40].
  - Stock page (as of Mar 31, 2026): "Stock code 6586 Stock exchange registration Tokyo Stock Exchange, Prime Market Nagoya Stock Exchange, Premier Market" [S41].
- **Canada.**
  - History: "Nov. 1973 Established Makita Power Tools Canada Ltd. in Canada. (Changed the name to Makita Canada Inc. in January 1991.)" [S43]
  - makita.ca footer: "Copyright © 2026 Makita Canada Inc All Rights Reserved - 1950 Forbes Street, Whitby, ON. L1N 7B7".
  - Contact page: "Head Office 1950 Forbes Street Whitby, Ontario L1N 7B7". Regional offices in Richmond, BC and St. Leonard, QC [S42].
- Makita is not owned by either SBD or TTI. No licence was found.

### 3.11 Bosch
- **Owner and parent.** Bosch Annual Report 2025, p. 6:
  - "The company was set up in Stuttgart in 1886 by Robert Bosch (1861–1942)…"
  - "Ninety-four percent of the share capital of Robert Bosch GmbH is held by Robert Bosch Stiftung GmbH, a company with limited liability and a charitable purpose. The remaining shares are held by Robert Bosch GmbH and a company owned by the Bosch family. The majority of voting rights are held by Robert Bosch Industrietreuhand KG." [S50]
  - Answer to the task question: ownership is "Robert Bosch Stiftung" for 94% of the share capital, with voting control at the Industrietreuhand KG. Splits beyond that are U14.
- **Canada.** bosch.ca corporate information: "Robert Bosch Inc. 6955 Creditview Rd, Mississauga, ON L5N 1R1, Canada"; footer "© Robert Bosch Inc. 2026" [S51]. boschtools.com/ca is the Canadian power-tools site, but its pages showed no street address [S52].

### 3.12 Kobalt (alternate)
- **Owner.** Lowe's FY2025 10-K (filed 2026-03-23): a Lowe's subsidiary "owns and maintains various additional registered and unregistered trademarks … including private brand product names, such as, "Kobalt", "STAINMASTER", and "allen+roth."" [S60]
- **Canada exit.** Lowe's FY2022 10-K: "On February 3, 2023, Lowe's completed the sale of its Canadian retail business, which operated 232 stores in Canada, as well as serviced 210 dealer-owned stores. The Canadian retail business included a number of complementary formats under the banners of RONA, Lowe's Canada, Réno-Dépôt, and Dick's Lumber." … "we … no longer operate in the Canada market" [S61]
- **Current availability in Canada: not confirmed.**
  - Flipp search "kobalt" for Toronto (M5B 2H1) on 2026-10-06 returned **no** flyer or e-commerce items [S70].
  - rona.ca returned HTTP 403 to our fetch.
  - Recommend not using Kobalt as an alternate (U6).

### 3.13 HART, Hyper Tough, Hercules (alternates)
- **HART.**
  - TTI segment note: products "are available under the MILWAUKEE, AEG, RYOBI, HOMELITE and HART brands" [S11].
  - Chairman's statement: "voluntary exit of the HART business (US$156 million in 2025)" and "one-time write-offs of intangible assets, mainly related to the HART exit" [S11].
  - The retailer link and Canadian availability are not verified. Flipp "hart drill" for M5B 2H1 returned nothing [S70] (U7).
- **Hyper Tough.** Flipp e-commerce results for M5B 2H1 on 2026-10-06, all merchant "Walmart" [S70]:
  - "Hyper Tough 20v Max Brushless Cordless Drill Driver | $64.98"
  - "Hyper Tough 12v Max Cordless Drill | $49.98"
  - walmart.ca itself blocked our fetch. The brand owner is not verified from a primary source (U8).
- **Hercules.** Flipp "hercules tool" for M5B 2H1 returned nothing [S70]. Owner and Canadian availability not verified (U9). Recommend not using.

## 6. UNVERIFIED / DO-NOT-USE

**UNVERIFIED.** Do not use on air unless re-verified from a primary source.

| ID | Claim | Status / why |
|---|---|---|
| U1 | "TTI makes RIDGID power tools" / "One World Technologies is a TTI subsidiary" / "TTI holds the RIDGID licence" | No TTI primary document names RIDGID or One World Technologies. TTI's 2025 subsidiary table has no "One World" entry. The only support is secondary sources and court-case captions ("One World Technologies, Inc. d/b/a Techtronic Industries Power Equipment", finnegan.com), which are not fetched. **Use the manual line instead:** "RIDGID, Inc., used under license", made by One World Technologies. |
| U2 | Ridge Tool founded 1923; became an Emerson subsidiary in 1966 | ridgid.com returned 403 (blocked). Search snippets only. |
| U3 | TTI paid US$626.6 million for Milwaukee, bought from Atlas Copco | Law-firm news item and search snippets only (S90, tier b). The year 2005 is primary [S14, S15]; the price and seller are not. |
| U4 | What "CTC Brand Corp." (Calgary) is, and its relationship to CTC | CIPO shows the 2026 assignment [S31]. CTC Brand Corp. does not appear in the AIF's principal-entity chart [S1]. Do not describe it as a subsidiary. |
| U5 | That CTC (or CTC Brand Corp.) owns the standalone "MAXIMUM" trademark | Only the "MASTERCRAFT MAXIMUM" word mark was checked. MAXIMUM is absent from the AIF Owned Brands lists. Safe wording: "Exclusive to Canadian Tire" [S30]. |
| U6 | Kobalt currently sold in Canada | No Flipp hits; rona.ca blocked. Forum claims that RONA was clearing Kobalt are not usable. |
| U7 | HART sold in Canada; HART made for or sold by Walmart | Not in TTI documents; walmart.ca blocked; no Flipp hits. |
| U8 | Hyper Tough is a Walmart-owned brand | Only Walmart e-commerce listings via Flipp [S70]. Owner not confirmed from a primary source. |
| U9 | Hercules (Harbor Freight) owner; not sold in Canada | No primary source fetched. |
| U10 | Robert Bosch Inc. (Mississauga) is the entity that distributes Bosch power tools in Canada | bosch.ca names Robert Bosch Inc. for the group's Canadian pages. boschtools.com/ca gave no address. Safe wording: "Bosch's Canadian company, Robert Bosch Inc., is in Mississauga." |
| U11 | Canadian office or distributor of RIDGID power tools | Not found. The manual and warranty addresses are in Anderson, South Carolina. |
| U12 | Bosch family voting-rights split ("about 7%"; shares "about 5% / 1%") | Secondary only. The AR gives 94% and "majority of voting rights", nothing more. |
| U13 | Lowe's Canada sale price (US$400 million, Sycamore Partners as buyer) | Secondary only; not in the captured 10-K text. |

**DO-NOT-USE.** Excluded by house rules.

| ID | Item | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| D1 | Any manufacturer or supplier name for Mastercraft, MAXIMUM or Powerfist. This includes forum threads such as "Look who is making tools for Canadian Tire" | House-brand maker rule. No own-brand primary page states a maker. |
| D2 | "RYOBI, the #1 global consumer cordless tool and outdoor products brand" (TTI); "the #1 Professional and Consumer brands in the World" (TTI CEO) | Performance and position claims. Quote only with attribution, and preferably not at all. |
| D3 | Milwaukee's "legendary durability" and "unmatched durability"; Craftsman's "DIY-ers most trusted brand*" | Durability and quality marketing claims. Never state as fact. |
| D4 | Recall and safety-notice links seen on ryobitools.ca ("Important Recall Information"), register.ridgidpower.com ("Important Recall Information") and dewalt.ca ("Safety Notices & Recalls") | Recalls go only on this list. Not researched; not for the script. |
| D5 | Wikipedia, SlashGear, "who owns X" blogs, ZoomInfo/PitchBook profiles, Grokipedia | Not primary (tier c). |
| D6 | Describing TTI as "Chinese" or "China's TTI" (SlashGear headline wording) | Use the primary wording "Hong Kong-based" (Ryobi Limited) or "incorporated in Hong Kong" (TTI AR). |
| D7 | SBD, TTI, Emerson or Lowe's revenue figures presented without "U.S. dollars" or as Canadian | U.S. figures rule. The only Canada-specific SBD figure is "Canada (4%)" of 2025 revenues [S2]. |
| D8 | Any suggestion that licensing, foreign ownership, or the Craftsman royalty arrangement is improper | Legal posture. These are disclosed commercial arrangements. Say so. |

**Count of UNVERIFIED items: 13 (U1–U13).**

## GAP FIXES (critic)

Added 2026-10-06 (captures around 01:35 UTC). Local copies are in `scratchpad/gap/`.

**GF-O1. RIDGID's Canadian contact page names Techtronic Industries Canada Inc. This resolves U11 and narrows U1 (tier a).**
- https://ca.powertools.ridgid.com/pages/contact-us, re-fetched today (gap/rid_contact.html): "Email ridgidcanada@ttigroupna.com Phone Number 1-800-268-4015 Address Techtronic Industries Canada Inc 3381 Steeles Ave E Suite 400 Toronto, ON M2H 3S7".
- That is the same street address as Milwaukee Tool Canada [S17], and the same phone number as Milwaukee Canada and RYOBI Canada [S17, S12b].
- What this proves: a TTI Canadian company is the contact for RIDGID power tools in Canada.
- What it does not prove: who owns the RIDGID trademark (RIDGID, Inc., Emerson subsidiary [S20, S22]), or who makes the tools.
- Script-safe line: "RIDGID's Canadian website lists its contact as Techtronic Industries Canada, at the same Toronto address as Milwaukee Tool Canada." Still do not say "TTI owns RIDGID".
- tl_record.md §5 calls TTI RIDGID's "operator". Use the contact-page wording above instead.

**GF-O2. Bosch Canada postal code conflict (both tier a).** bosch.ca corporate information gives "6955 Creditview Rd, Mississauga, ON L5N 1R1". The Bosch power-tools imprint (https://www.bosch-pt.com/ptlegalpages/ca/rbca/en/as/imprint, re-fetched today) gives "6955 Creditview Rd, Mississauga, ON L5N 8E9". Say "Mississauga" on air and give no postal code.

**GF-O3. CIPO "MASTERCRAFT MAXIMUM" dates.** The change in title is dated 2026-01-04 and was registered 2026-01-27 (tl_own/cipo_1643497.html). Both dates are correct; they are different fields.

**GF-O4. MAXIMUM ownership is still not primary-sourced (U5 stays open).**
- No standalone "MAXIMUM" Canadian mark was retrieved. A web search surfaced a U.S. record (uspto.report, "MAXIMUM – Canadian Tire Corporation, Limited", 87311869). That is a U.S. third-party mirror (tier d) and must not be used as Canadian proof.
- The brief's phrase "both Canadian Tire Corp owned brands" holds for Mastercraft only [S1]. For MAXIMUM, say "Exclusive to Canadian Tire" [S30].
- The MAXIMUM warranty page (tl_warranty GF-W1) says "MAXIMUM Canada will maintain this product". It names no owner and no maker. Do not infer one.


======================================================================

# DOSSIER: WARRANTY TERMS, 10 TOOL BRANDS SOLD IN CANADA

Project: The Canadian Counter, "We Investigated 10 Tool Brands Sold in Canada (Only 1 Covers Your Cordless Drill for Life)", uploading 2026-10-07.
Scope: verbatim warranty terms from each brand's Canadian page (or the Canadian retailer's page for house brands), plus a test of the key claim.
All captures were made on **2026-10-06**. Local copies are in `scratchpad/tlw/`.
Tiers: (a) primary (brand's own Canadian page, or the Canadian retailer's own product or policy page); (b) named outlet; (c) do-not-use (forums, US-only pages used for Canadian claims, conflicting retailer fields); (d) survey.

---

## 0. BOTTOM LINE ON THE KEY CLAIM

**Claim tested:** "RIDGID's Lifetime Service Agreement (LSA) is the ONLY lifetime coverage on cordless power tools among the 10 brands in Canada."

**Result: the claim held up against every Canadian brand or retailer page checked on 2026-10-06, with four qualifications the script must keep.**

1. **RIDGID's lifetime coverage is conditional.** It is an "agreement" you activate. It is not an automatic warranty. RIDGID's Canadian page says: "Activate your Lifetime Service Agreement within 90 days of purchase to get the benefits." The registration steps are "1. Create an account 2. Enter the model & serial number 3. Upload receipt". Without registration, the RIDGID registration site says the automatic coverage is a "3-year limited manufacturer warranty".
2. **RIDGID's own registration site calls the LSA "a promotional program"** and also uses the phrase "Limited Lifetime Service Agreement". Quote these words exactly. Do not upgrade them to "lifetime warranty". (Source R4, R5.)
3. **Princess Auto's ROYAL SERVICE Promise has no stated time limit.** It reads: "No sale is final until you're satisfied. We guarantee to make it right. We will repair, replace or refund any product to your satisfaction." It does not use the word "lifetime" and it is a retailer promise covering every product. No Powerfist cordless-tool warranty term appears on the Princess Auto product pages we checked. The script should not call this lifetime coverage. It also should not say that Powerfist has "no" long-term coverage. Recommended wording: "Princess Auto's product page lists no warranty length for this drill. It shows the store's ROYAL SERVICE Promise instead, and that promise names no time limit."
4. **Every "lifetime" found at the other brands applies to hand tools or non-powered items.** One exception is a single Milwaukee component, the LED in its LED Work Light. DeWalt's hand-tool limited lifetime warranty states that it does "not to exceed one year for products with electrical or electronic components".

**Safe title and thumbnail phrasing.** "ONLY 1 FOR LIFE" is supportable if the video says, near the claim, that the coverage needs registration within 90 days with a receipt. The phrase "Industry's Only Lifetime Service Agreement" is the wording on Home Depot Canada's RIDGID product listing. Attribute it to that listing. Do not adopt it as the channel's own finding, because we checked 10 brands plus alternates, not the whole industry.

---

## 1. RIDGID (Home Depot Canada exclusive)

**Sold in Canada:** yes. On 2026-10-06, Home Depot Canada product 1001592203 (RIDGID R86001K 18V 1/2-in Drill/Driver Kit) showed 7 in stock at store 7080, Gerrard Square, Toronto (R13). The Canadian RIDGID site says "Sold Exclusively At The Home Depot" (R1).

**Canadian LSA page, verbatim (R1, https://ca.powertools.ridgid.com/pages/lifetime-service-agreement):**
- "The industry’s best coverage"
- "FREE Batteries. FREE Parts. FREE Service. FOR LIFE."
- "HOW TO REGISTER. Activate your Lifetime Service Agreement within 90 days of purchase to get the benefits. Registration takes less than 5 minutes in 3 easy steps for a Lifetime of Coverage."
- "1. Create an account 2. Enter the model & serial number 3. Upload receipt"
- "FOR QUESTIONS OR CONCERNS REGARDING LSA, CONTACT US AT: 1-800-268-4015"
- French page (R2): "Piles GRATUITES. Pièces GRATUITES. Service GRATUIT. À VIE." / "Activez votre contrat de service à vie dans les 90 jours suivant l'achat…"

**Home Depot Canada listing, R86001K drill kit (R13):**
- Feature bullet: "Lifetime Service Agreement with registration within 90 days of purchase"
- Description: "With registration, this tool is backed by the Industry's Only Lifetime Service Agreement: FREE Parts. FREE Service. For LIFE."
- Kit contents: "(1) R86001 18V 1/2 in. Drill/Driver, (1) R87002 18V 2.0 Ah Battery, (1) R86093 18V Charger…"
- Warranty field: "For more information please contact customer service at 1-800-628-0525"

**Home Depot Canada RIDGID brand page (R12):** "Our Lifetime Service Agreement means that any registered power tool or battery that fails with regular use, will be repaired or replaced, for free." / "PURCHASE Buy a RIDGID power tool, battery or charger. Keep your receipt for RIDGID Registration. REGISTER Collect your purchase receipt and product serial number, as both are required to activate your Lifetime Service Agreement."

**Batteries and chargers, including ones sold separately (Home Depot Canada, R14):**
- AC840060PN 6.0 Ah 2-pack. Warranty field: "Lifetime Service Agreement with registration within 90 days of purchase". Description: "these batteries are eligible for the Industry’s Best Lifetime Service Agreement, simply register within 90 days of purchase for FREE Parts, FREE Service, For LIFE"
- AC840060 6.0 Ah single. Warranty field: "3-Year Limited Warranty. For more details on this warranty, please call 1-866-539-1710". Description: "this battery is eligible for the Industrys Best Lifetime Service Agreement, simply register within 90 days of purchase…"
- AC86093N charger. Warranty field: "Lifetime Service Agreement with registration within 90 days of purchase. For more details on this warranty, please call 1-866-539-1710"; also "Lifetime Service Agreement: Free parts and service for life with registration"
- **CONFLICT (see DO-NOT-USE):** The RIDGID registration-site FAQ (R5) lists "accessory batteries, accessory chargers" among the categories "not eligible for coverage under the LSA". Batteries and chargers sold inside a tool kit are covered, according to the same site. For standalone batteries, Home Depot Canada's listing and the registration-site FAQ disagree.

**Registration site, register.ridgidpower.com (shared North American site; it shows US mailing addresses) (R4, R5):**
- FAQ: "All RIDGID Power Tool products come with an automatic 3-year limited manufacturer warranty. The Lifetime Service Agreement (LSA) is a promotional program (that applies to eligible product categories) upon registration completion. To take advantage of the LSA ’s benefits, simply register your eligible RIDGID power tool product and submit your proof of purchase for validation within 90 days of the date of purchase. No additional fees, no hassle."
- Eligible: "Eligible categories for the Ridgid LSA include Ridgid brand cordless power tools, corded power tools, tile saws, bench and stationary power tools, and pneumatic products."
- Not eligible: "…any free or promotional serialized piece of equipment provided beyond the contents of the purchased tool kit, generators, pressure washers, accessory batteries, accessory chargers, plumbing equipment, extension cords, heated gear, storage, hand tools, and wet/dry vacs."
- Replacements: "Any time a serialized piece of equipment is replaced, the new product will have a new serial code and will need to be re-registered in order to carry over the LSA coverage… Re-registration must occur within 90 days of replacement. Proof of replacement may be required."
- About LSA: "All eligible RIDGID ® Brand hand held power tools, stationary power tools and pneumatic tools purchased from The Home Depot and authorized retailers receive the Limited Lifetime Service Agreement with registration. Free Parts. Free Service. For Life."
- The 3-year limited warranty without LSA: "All RIDGID ® Brand Hand Held Power Tools, Stationary Power Tools, and Pneumatic Tools are automatically covered under a 3-year Limited Warranty." It covers "normal wear items such as brushes, chucks, motors, switches, gears and even cordless batteries". Not covered: "any malfunction, failure or defect resulting from misuse, abuse, alteration, or repair by anyone other than an authorized service center."
- **Hand tools:** RIDGID has a "LIMITED LIFETIME AGREEMENT" for "concrete and masonry hand tools and RIDGID ® tile hand tools" (trowels, floats, nippers and similar). It excludes "damage resulting from normal wear and tear".
- Commercial-use exclusion: none found on the pages checked.
- "Limited": the 3-year is "Limited". The LSA is called "Limited Lifetime Service Agreement" on the registration site and "Lifetime Service Agreement" on the Canadian page.
- 90-day or 1-year offers: none found on RIDGID's Canadian page.

**Answers to the brief's four RIDGID questions:**
- Does the LSA apply in Canada? **Yes.** The Canadian site (ca.powertools.ridgid.com) has its own LSA page with a Canadian phone number, 1-800-268-4015, and Home Depot Canada listings carry the LSA.
- Is registration within 90 days required? **Yes.** "Activate your Lifetime Service Agreement within 90 days of purchase."
- Does it cover batteries and chargers? **Kit batteries and chargers: yes.** The Canadian page says "FREE Batteries". **Standalone batteries and chargers: the sources conflict.** Home Depot Canada says eligible; the registration-site FAQ says "accessory batteries, accessory chargers" are not.
- Is it free parts and service for life? The Canadian page says "FREE Batteries. FREE Parts. FREE Service. FOR LIFE." Quote that line and attribute it to RIDGID.

## 2. RYOBI (Home Depot Canada)

**Sold in Canada:** yes. Home Depot Canada lists PCL206K1X, 1001686659 (R15).
**Source:** ryobitools.ca/pages/warranties links the "18V ONE+" power-tool warranty to a trilingual PDF hosted on the Canadian site's CDN (R6). The PDF names One World Technologies, Anderson SC, and refers to "state law". It is US-origin text served by the Canadian site.
- Title: "RYOBI® POWER TOOL - LIMITED THREE YEAR WARRANTY AND 90 DAY EXCHANGE POLICY"
- Cordless tool: "This warranty covers all defects in workmanship or materials in your RYOBI ® power tool for a period of three years from the date of purchase."
- Battery: "With the exception of batteries, power tool accessories are warranted for ninety (90) days. Batteries are warranted for three years."
- Charger: not separately stated in the PDF.
- 90-day: "90-DAY EXCHANGE POLICY: During the first 90 days after date of purchase, you may either request service under this warranty or you may exchange any RYOBI ® power tool which does not work properly due to defective workmanship or materials by returning the power tool to the dealer from which it was purchased."
- Proof of purchase: "When you request warranty service, you must also present proof of purchase documentation, which includes the date of purchase (for example, a bill of sale)."
- Exclusions: "This warranty applies only to the original purchaser at retail and may not be transferred. This warranty only covers defects arising under normal usage and does not cover any malfunction, failure or defects resulting from misuse, abuse, neglect, alteration, modification or repairs by other than Authorized Service Centers."
- Registration: none required in the warranty text.
- Home Depot Canada warranty field for PCL206K1X: "3-Year manufacture Warranty. For more information contact 1-800-525-2579" (R15).
- **Hand tools (non-powered):** "Limited Lifetime Warranty… The RYOBI PRODUCT will be free from defects in materials and workmanship for the usable life of the original product". It applies to "Tape Measures, Hammers, Shovels, etc." and excludes "Damage resulting from normal wear and tear and Acts of God" (R7).

## 3. DEWALT

**Sold in Canada:** yes. Home Depot Canada lists DCK277D2 (R16) and Canadian Tire lists 0542320P (R30).
**Source:** dewalt.ca/en-ca/support/warranty (R8).
- Cordless tools: "Three-Year Limited Warranty. For all DEWALT ® products stating they come with a 3-year limited warranty, 1 year free service contract, and 90 day money back guarantee, DEWALT will repair, without charge, any defects due to faulty materials or workmanship for three years from the date of purchase, or date of delivery of the product (whichever date is later)."
- Exclusions: "This warranty does not cover part failure due to normal wear or tool abuse." / "Because DEWALT cannot control the quality of products sold by unauthorized sellers, this warranty applies only if the product was purchased from DEWALT or a DEWALT authorized seller, unless otherwise prohibited by law." / "This warranty does not apply to accessories or damage caused where repairs have been made or attempted by others."
- Transferability: "this warranty is extended only to the original end-user purchaser of the product and is not transferable" (with the carve-out "Except where prohibited by applicable law (which may include the Province of Quebec)").
- Proof of purchase: "Proof of purchase showing when and where you purchased the product may be required."
- 1-year free service: "One-Year Free Service Contract. DEWALT will maintain the tool and replace worn parts caused by normal use, for free, any time during the first year after purchase, or date of delivery of the product (whichever date is later)."
- 90-day money back: "90-Day Money Back Guarantee. If you are not completely satisfied with the performance of your DEWALT Power Tool, Laser, or Nailer for any reason, you can return it within 90 days from the date of purchase, or date of delivery of the product (whichever date is later) for a full refund."
- Batteries: the dewalt.ca product pages for the DCB205 20V MAX 5Ah battery and the DCB124-2 12V MAX 3Ah battery 2-pack both read "3 Year Limited Warranty, 1 Year Free Service, 90 Days Satisfaction Guaranteed". The DCD794D1 drill kit page reads the same (R9).
- A 7-year limited warranty also exists for "all DEWALT ® products stating they come with a 7 year limited warranty". Which products carry it was not identified, so it is UNVERIFIED for any cordless drill.
- Registration: none required.
- **Hand tools:** "DEWALT Mechanics Tools are covered by the manufacturer’s FULL LIFETIME WARRANTY. If any DEWALT Mechanics Tool ever fails for any reason, DEWALT will replace it. NO PROOF OF PURCHASE required." Covered: "wrenches, sockets, ratchets and drive accessories." The DeWalt hand-tool limited lifetime warranty runs "for as long as the original end-user purchaser owns the product (not to exceed one year for products with electrical or electronic components)".
- Home Depot Canada warranty field for DCK277D2: "3 Year Limited Warranty. 1 Year Free Service. For more information please contact customer service at 1-800-628-0525" (R16).

## 4. MILWAUKEE

**Sold in Canada:** yes. Home Depot Canada lists 3601-22CT, 1001824335 (R17).
**Source:** milwaukeetool.ca/Support/Registration-and-Warranty (R10).
- Cordless tool: "Every MILWAUKEE power tool* (see exceptions below) is warranted to the original purchaser only to be free from defects in material and workmanship. Subject to certain exceptions, MILWAUKEE will repair or replace any part on an electric power tool which, after examination, is determined by MILWAUKEE to be defective in material or workmanship for a period of five (5) years** after the date of purchase unless otherwise noted."
- Batteries are carved out of the tool warranty: "*This warranty does not cover … Cordless Battery Packs; … Hand Tools; …"
- Battery table (lithium-ion, from date of purchase): "Three (3) Years" for 48-11-1850, 48-11-1860, 48-11-1865, 48-11-1880, 48-11-1890 and others. "Two (2) Years" for 48-11-1815, 48-11-1820, 48-11-1835 and others.
- Charger: not separately stated on the Canadian page. Home Depot Canada's field for 3601-22CT reads: "5 year limited tool warranty, 5 year limited charger warranty and 2 year limited battery warranty" (R17).
- Registration: "Warranty Registration is not necessary to obtain the applicable warranty on a MILWAUKEE power tool product. The manufacturing date of the product will be used to determine the warranty period if no proof of purchase is provided at the time warranty service is requested."
- Exclusions: "This warranty does not apply to damage that MILWAUKEE determines to be from repairs made or attempted by anyone other than MILWAUKEE authorized personnel, misuse, alterations, abuse, normal wear and tear, lack of maintenance, or accidents." / "Normal Wear : … This warranty does not cover repair when normal use has exhausted the life of a part including, but not limited to, chucks, brushes, cords…"
- Territory: "This warranty applies to product sold in the U.S.A. and Canada only."
- **Lifetime items (not cordless tools):** "The warranty period for the LED in the LED Work Light and the LED Upgrade Bulb for the Work Light is the lifetime of the product subject to the limitations above." Hand tools: "MILWAUKEE will repair or replace any hand tool which, after examination, is determined by MILWAUKEE to be defective in material or workmanship for the lifetime of the tool." There is also a "LIFETIME GUARANTEE" for products that carry it: "replaceable upon failure over the entire life of the product, excluding misuse & abuse".
- 90-day or 1-year offers: none on the page.

## 5. MAKITA

**Sold in Canada:** yes. Home Depot Canada lists DHP487Y005 (R18). Makita Canada Inc. is at Whitby, ON.
**Source:** makita.ca "Makita Canada Inc. - Warranty Statement" (R11).
- Title: "Limited Three Year Pneumatic Nailer, Lithium Ion Tool, Battery and Charger Warranty"
- Cordless tool, battery and charger: "All Makita lithium ion products, lithium ion batteries and lithium ion battery chargers are warranted to be free of defects from workmanship and materials for a period of THREE YEARS from the date of original purchase. Exclusions are, but not limited to, flashlights, work lights, job site radios, speakers, jackets/vests, power source adapters and battery adapters. Products excluded have a ONE YEAR warranty."
- Date note: "The limited THREE YEAR warranty for Pneumatic nailers, staplers as well as Lithium Ion batteries and chargers applies only to products purchased in Canada after April 1, 2018."
- General: "All Makita tools purchased in Canada from authorized dealers are warranted to be free of defects from workmanship and materials for a period of ONE YEAR from the date of original purchase."
- **Non-Makita batteries clause, verbatim:** "Regarding lithium ion products and chargers; the use of non-Makita brand batteries will void all warranties for these products."
- Other exclusions: "Repairs to the product have been made or attempted by others." / "Repairs to the product are required because of normal wear and tear." / "The product has been abused, misused or improperly maintained." / "Alterations have been made to the product."
- Commercial use: the outdoor power equipment paragraph says "This warranty includes tools used in consumer, commercial or rental applications." No commercial exclusion was stated for the lithium-ion tools.
- Proof of purchase and registration: the statement sets no registration requirement. Proof of purchase is not explicitly stated on the page.
- Also: "This warranty is subject to change without notice."
- **Not a warranty:** Makita Canada's "Maximum Repair Program" is a capped *paid* repair, "based on 40% of the current list price for the tool model". Do not present it as free or lifetime coverage. It is unrelated to Canadian Tire's MAXIMUM brand.
- Hand-tool lifetime: none found on Makita Canada's warranty statement.
- Home Depot Canada field for DHP487Y005 repeats the three-year sentence (R18).

## 6. BOSCH

**Sold in Canada:** yes. Home Depot Canada lists GSR18V-400B22 (R19) and Canadian Tire lists 0548811P and 0548727P (R31).
**Source:** boschtools.com/ca/en/service/product-warranty/ (R20). **Correction to the brief: Bosch 18V is NOT 1 year for current purchases.**
- Applies to "Date of purchase 6/1/23 & later". Title: "Limited Warranty of Bosch 18V Portable Power Tools, 18V Benchtop Power Tools, 18V Chargers and 18V Batteries"
- Terms: "18V Cordless Power Tools, 18V Benchtop Power Tools and 18V Chargers Five (5) years from the date of purchase" / "18V Power Tool Batteries Three (3) years from the date of purchase"
- 12V: "12V Cordless Power Tools and 12V Chargers Three (3) years from the date of purchase" / "12V Power Tool Batteries Three (3) years from the date of purchase"
- Before 6/1/23 and for corded tools: one year.
- Exclusions: "WHAT IS NOT COVERED: (1) Damage caused by use of non-genuine Bosch parts is not covered by this Limited Warranty. (2) Damage caused by repairs done by any person other than Bosch or a Bosch Authorized Service Center is not covered by this Limited Warranty. (3) Normal wear and tear of your Product is not covered by this Limited Warranty."
- Proof of purchase: "Please include a dated proof of purchase with your Product so we can determine if your Product is within the Warranty Period. If you do not have the dated proof of purchase … we will use the Product’s production code…"
- Territory: "THIS LIMITED WARRANTY APPLIES ONLY TO PRODUCTS SOLD WITHIN THE UNITED STATES OF AMERICA AND CANADA."
- Registration extension: this applies **only to lasers and measuring tools**, not drills: "Bosch will extend warranty coverage to two (2) years when you register your product within eight (8) weeks after date of purchase." Lasers and measuring tools also have a "30 Day Money Back Refund or Replacement".
- 180-day return: "PROFACTOR™ GUARANTEE … Return guarantee applicable to all Bosch 18V PROFACTOR™ cordless tools… Returns only acceptable at original point of purchase within 180 days after the date of purchase." The covered list is circular saws, grinders, impact wrenches, rotary hammers, miter saws, table saws and track saws, which does not include drills (R21).
- **Retailer listings conflict with Bosch's own page (see DO-NOT-USE):** Home Depot Canada lists GSR18V-400B22 with the warranty field "1 Year". Canadian Tire lists 0548811P with "This product carries a 1 year exchange warranty redeemable at any Canadian Tire store." The Canadian Tire line describes an in-store exchange term and is not Bosch's manufacturer warranty.
- Hand-tool lifetime: none found.

## 7. CRAFTSMAN

**Sold in Canada: yes, but not at Canadian Tire or Home Depot Canada in our searches.**
- craftsman.ca's product-registration form lists "placeOfPurchase": ["RONA","KENT","Amazon"] (R22).
- Flipp (postal code M5B 2H1, 2026-10-06) showed a RONA & RONA+ flyer item: "CRAFTSMAN Drill and impact driver combo | $129 | was 199 | SAVE 35% | valid 2026-10-01..2026-10-15". Use this only as evidence the brand is sold. It is not script-ready as a price, because the model, the store and the unit price are missing.
- RONA's own pages returned HTTP 403 to our fetch, so we have no direct retailer-page capture. See UNVERIFIED.

**Source:** craftsman.ca/en-ca/support/customer-support/warranty (R23), with product pages R24 and R25.
- Cordless: "3 Year Limited Warranty For three years from the date of sale, the product is warrantied against defects in material or workmanship. Cordless (V20) Hand Held Power Tools Sold After September 2018 (Model # starting with "CMC")"
- V20 drill CMCD700C1 product page: "3 Year Limited Warranty" (R24).
- V20 battery CMCB202-2 product page: "3 Year Limited Warranty" (R25).
- 90-day: "Note: 90 Day Satisfaction Guarantee". This note sits under the *corded* handheld power tools block and also under compressors and pneumatic nailers. It does **not** appear in the V20 cordless block.
- Proof of purchase: "Proof of purchase is required." This appears in the corded block. The V20 block's text was not captured beyond the line above.
- Registration: optional ("Unlock benefits by registering your product").
- **Hand tools, the falsification candidate:** "Full lifetime warranty If the product fails to perform for any reason, we will replace it." This covers hammers, pliers, ratchets and sockets, screwdrivers, wrenches and similar tools. "No proof of purchase required. Return damaged product to a stocking Retail Partner or contact Customer Care for details." Mechanics tools carry the same full lifetime warranty.
- Other limited-lifetime categories: electronic measuring tools (lasers, stud finders), tape measures, portable plastic storage and gloves.
- Commercial exclusion, on "Other hand tools" and torque wrenches: "This warranty applies for only 90 days from the date of sale if this product is ever used while providing commercial services or if rented to another person."
- **Conclusion:** Craftsman's lifetime coverage is on hand tools only. Its V20 cordless coverage is 3 years limited.

## 10. POWERFIST (Princess Auto house brand)

No manufacturer or supplier is named or inferred.

**Sold in Canada:** yes. Princess Auto product pages, all captured 2026-10-06 (R29):
- PA0009048984, "20V 1/2 in. Cordless Drill/Driver, Tool Only", Brand Name: Powerfist. Shown as "Available".
- PA0009486457, "20V Cordless Drill Driver".
- PA0009054685, "20V 1/2 in. Li-ion Cordless Hammer Drill Kit".

**Warranty:**
- No warranty length for the tool, battery or charger appears in the rendered text of these three product pages. A raw-HTML search for "warrant" on all four pages fetched also returned nothing.
- The pages show the store-wide promise: "Our ROYAL SERVICE Promise No sale is final until you're satisfied. We guarantee to make it right. We will repair, replace or refund any product to your satisfaction."
- The FAQ repeats it: "This is your guarantee from Princess Auto. “No sale is final until you're satisfied. We guarantee to make it right. We will repair, replace or refund any product to your satisfaction.”" (R29b)
- Receipt: "If you are making a return or exchange, and have misplaced your receipt, all we need to do is look up your information and the appropriate purchase in order to process the return or exchange." This is from the Princess Auto returns page, captured 2026-09-08 (`ct/pa_returns.md`). Re-check it before use.
- Registration: none stated.
- Hand tools: no Princess Auto primary page found stating a lifetime term. Forum posts claiming one are tier c.
- **Script handling:** say the product page lists no warranty length and shows the ROYAL SERVICE Promise. Do not call it lifetime coverage, and do not call it "no warranty".

| Brand | Cordless tool term | Battery term | Registration | Key exclusion | Source URL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **RIDGID** | 3-yr limited automatic. **Lifetime Service Agreement** if registered ("FREE Batteries. FREE Parts. FREE Service. FOR LIFE.") | Kit batteries: covered by the LSA. Standalone batteries: sources conflict (HD Canada says eligible; registration FAQ says "accessory batteries" are not) | **Required within 90 days, with receipt upload**; re-register replacements within 90 days | Misuse, abuse, alteration, non-authorized repair; registration-site FAQ excludes accessory batteries and chargers | https://ca.powertools.ridgid.com/pages/lifetime-service-agreement |

| DeWalt | 3-yr limited + 1-yr free service + 90-day money back | 3 yr (product pages) | None | "normal wear or tool abuse"; unauthorized sellers | https://www.dewalt.ca/en-ca/support/warranty |
| Milwaukee | 5-yr | 2 or 3 yr by battery model | Not necessary | Normal wear and tear, misuse, abuse, accidents | https://www.milwaukeetool.ca/support/registration-and-warranty |
| Makita | 3-yr limited (lithium-ion) | 3 yr | None stated | "use of non-Makita brand batteries will void all warranties"; normal wear and tear | https://www.makita.ca/index2new.php?event=productwarrenty&event=productwarrenty |
| Bosch | 5-yr limited for 18V tools and chargers (purchases from 6/1/23); 3-yr for 12V | 3 yr | None for tools (registration extension is for lasers only) | Non-genuine Bosch parts; normal wear and tear | https://www.boschtools.com/ca/en/service/product-warranty/ |
| Craftsman | 3-yr limited (V20) | 3 yr limited (CMCB202-2) | Optional | Not shown for V20; commercial use cuts some hand-tool warranties to 90 days | https://www.craftsman.ca/en-ca/support/customer-support/warranty |
| Mastercraft (CT) | 3-yr exchange at Canadian Tire | 2-yr exchange (PWR POD batteries and charger) | None | Receipt: "warranties on an item without a receipt may not be accepted" | https://www.canadiantire.ca/ (product warrantyMessage, e.g. 0548703P) |
| MAXIMUM (CT) | Varies: 5-yr limited (brushless kits), or 1-yr repair-only (older kits); some say "special warranty" | 3–5 yr, inconsistent by listing | None | "partially repairable"; Canadian Tire receipt policy | https://www.canadiantire.ca/ (product warrantyMessage, e.g. 0547970P, 0543196P) |
| Powerfist (PA) | Not stated on product page; ROYAL SERVICE Promise has no stated limit | Not stated | None | Not stated | https://www.princessauto.com/en/product/PA0009048984 |
| Black+Decker (alt) | 1-yr exchange (CT, BDCDD120C-2) or 3-yr repair (CT, BDC120VACA) | Not verified | Optional | "commercial use… will void this warranty" | https://www.blackanddecker.ca/warranty-policy |

**Hand-tool "lifetime", recorded separately (none of these covers a cordless drill):**
- Craftsman: hand and mechanics tools carry a "Full lifetime warranty", no proof of purchase required.
- DeWalt: mechanics tools carry a "FULL LIFETIME WARRANTY". Other hand tools carry a limited lifetime warranty capped at one year for anything electrical.
- Milwaukee: hand tools carry a limited lifetime warranty, plus a "LIFETIME GUARANTEE" on products stating it. The LED in the LED Work Light has a lifetime term.
- Ryobi: non-powered hand tools carry a limited lifetime warranty.
- RIDGID: concrete, masonry and tile hand tools carry a limited lifetime warranty.
- Mastercraft and MAXIMUM: hand tools carry a lifetime limited exchange warranty.
- Black+Decker: a "Lifetime Limited Warranty" text exists, but the products it applies to are not stated.
- Makita, Bosch and Powerfist: none found.

## 13. UNVERIFIED / DO-NOT-USE

**Recalls and safety notices:** none were collected for this dossier. RIDGID, DeWalt, Craftsman and B+D pages link to recall pages. Those were not opened and nothing from them goes in the script.

**Unverified (keep out of the script unless re-checked):**
1. Standalone RIDGID batteries and chargers under the LSA. Home Depot Canada listings (AC840060PN, AC840060, AC86093N, AC9840) say eligible. The registration-site FAQ says "accessory batteries, accessory chargers" are not. Say only what each source says, attributed to it, or limit the claim to kit batteries.
2. Whether RIDGID's "Service Direct" program (ship-in with a FedEx label, "as little as 14 days") operates in Canada. It appears only on the shared registration site and the US page.
3. The text inside RIDGID's Canadian LSA page FAQ accordion: "We offer a 30-day return policy…", "Yes, we ship to most countries worldwide…". It reads as template filler and has nothing to do with the LSA. **Tier c. Do not quote.**
4. US RIDGID LSA page (powertools.ridgid.com) items such as the "Covered / Not Covered" list. These are US-page statements. Do not present them as Canadian terms. (Tier c for Canadian claims.)
5. Bosch 18V 5-year coverage against the retailer fields: Home Depot Canada ("1 Year") and Canadian Tire ("1 year exchange warranty redeemable at any Canadian Tire store"). Use Bosch's own page. If the retailer figures are mentioned, label the Canadian Tire one as an in-store exchange term.
6. MAXIMUM listings that combine "5 year limited warranty" with "1 yr tool repair; 3 yr battery/charger repair". The text contradicts itself, so don't quote a single MAXIMUM term as the brand's warranty. Quote a specific listing with its CT product number.
7. DeWalt's 7-year limited warranty: which products carry it was not identified.
8. Milwaukee charger term: it is stated only on the Home Depot Canada field ("5 year limited charger warranty"), not on Milwaukee's Canadian page.
9. Craftsman retail availability: the RONA product page was blocked (403). Evidence is only the craftsman.ca place-of-purchase list (RONA, KENT, Amazon) and a RONA flyer item on Flipp (valid 2026-10-01 to 2026-10-15). Not script-ready as a price.
10. Kobalt, Hart and Hyper Tough warranty terms in Canada, and Hart's availability in Canada. The search-engine summaries (Kobalt "5-year tool / 3-year battery"; Hart "3-year limited") came from US or unofficial pages. **Tier c.**
11. Powerfist hand-tool lifetime warranty: forum claims only (RedFlagDeals, GarageJournal). **Tier c.**
12. Mastercraft "no receipt" exchange experiences on forums (RedFlagDeals, GarageJournal, bc4x4, canadiantiresucks.net). **Tier c.** Use only Canadian Tire's policy line.
13. "Industry's Only" and "Industry's Best": RIDGID and Home Depot Canada marketing wording. Do not state it as the channel's finding.
14. Ryobi's Canadian-site warranty PDF is US-origin text (Anderson SC, "state law"). Quote it as "the warranty Ryobi Canada's site links to". Do not describe it as a Canada-specific document.
15. Makita "Maximum Repair Program": paid, capped-cost repair at 40% of list price. It is not a warranty and not lifetime. Do not confuse it with the MAXIMUM brand.
16. No durability or quality inference may be drawn from warranty length (house rule). Do not say or imply that a longer warranty means a better or longer-lasting tool.

## GAP FIXES (critic)

Added 2026-10-06 by the completeness critic. Every capture below was taken between 01:30 and 01:45 UTC on 2026-10-06, which is the evening of 2026-10-05 in Toronto. Local copies are in `scratchpad/gap/`. The source IDs G1 to G12 are listed at the end of this section.

**GF-W1. MAXIMUM has a brand-level warranty page on canadiantire.ca. It answers the "special warranty" listings (tier a, G1).**
- The page title is "MAXIMUM Warranty | Canadian Tire", URL https://www.canadiantire.ca/en/all-brands/maximum/warranty.html. The MAXIMUM brand page links to it with "A warranty you can count on… Learn More".
- Under the heading "CORLESS POWER TOOLS" (Canadian Tire's spelling; quote it exactly or paraphrase as "cordless power tools") the page reads, verbatim:
  - "90 DAYS SATISFACTION GUARANTEE If you are not completely satisfied with the performance of your MAXIMUM product, it can be returned within 90 days from the date of purchase, with proof of purchase, for exchange or a full refund."
  - "UP TO 1 YEAR REPAIR & SERVICE WARRANTY MAXIMUM Canada will maintain this product, offering tune-ups and replacing critical parts that have worn beyond reasonable use due to normal use of such product, any time during the first year after purchase."
  - "UP TO 5 YEARS LIMITED WARRANTY These MAXIMUM products will continue to have a limited warranty against defects in workmanship and materials after the 1 year warranty repair period. This limited warranty excludes wear or breakage due to misuse and/or abuse or normal wear and tear."
  - "5 PLUS YEARS POST-WARRANTY SERVICING MAXIMUM will continue to support customers with these tools at authorized service centres across the country after the first year from purchase date with servicing for a fee."
- "LIFETIME WARRANTY" ("a no time limit warranty") appears on this page only for MANUAL CUTTING & MEASURING TOOLS, MANUAL FASTENING TOOLS, METAL WORKING TOOLS and SOCKETS & WRENCHES.
- **Effect on the key claim:** MAXIMUM's own warranty page caps its cordless power tools at "UP TO 5 YEARS". That closes the gap left by the two MAXIMUM drill kits whose listings show only "This product carries a special warranty" (0548799P and 4994235P, see GF-W2).
- **Effect on ranking:** MAXIMUM also has a 90-day satisfaction guarantee and a 1-year repair and service warranty. Those are tie-break-2 extras that the retention ranking did not credit. Say "up to five years". Do not say "five years" for every MAXIMUM kit, because 0543196P still reads "1-year limited repair only warranty".

**GF-W2. A fresh sweep of every Mastercraft and MAXIMUM cordless drill kit at Canadian Tire store 150 (tier a, G2).**
I fetched 17 product-family records today. These are all the house-brand cordless drill kits that CT search returned for "cordless drill", "mastercraft/maximum cordless drill", "hammer drill", "drill kit" and "combo kit".
- Mastercraft, 10 kits (0547546P, 0547551P, 0547578P [rotary hammer drill, tool only], 0548500P, 0548504P, 0548703P, 0548714P, 0548737P, 0548774P, 0548778P): every one reads "This product carries a 3 year exchange warranty redeemable at any Canadian Tire store." The 0548703P manual reads "guaranteed for a period of three (3) years", with "Batteries, chargers and carrying case… two (2) years".
- MAXIMUM, 7 kits:
  - 0547970P, 0547987P and 0547988P: "5 year limited warranty against defects in workmanship and material."
  - 0541349P: the self-contradicting "5 year limited… 1 yr tool repair; 3 yr battery/charger repair".
  - 0543196P: "1-year limited repair only warranty".
  - 0548799P and 4994235P: "special warranty", with no manual linked (`productManualLink: null`).
- **Not one house-brand cordless drill listing carries a lifetime term.**

**GF-W3. Powerfist owner's manuals exist and state no warranty (tier a, G3).** This corrects tl_platform A7 and E14, which say "No owner's manual was linked".
- Princess Auto's product pages link manuals hosted on its media server:
  - https://media-endpoint-prod-dvdgaaehgmc5dsa3.a01.azurefd.net/media-assets-prod/documents/9048984_manualhb_00_01_v01_manuel_enfr.pdf (20V 1/2 in. drill/driver, 41 pages, EN/FR)
  - …/9054685_manualhb_00_01_v01_manuel_enfr.pdf (20V hammer drill kit, 45 pages, EN/FR)
- A full-text search of both manuals for "warrant", "garantie" and "guarantee" returned **zero** hits.
- The only service line is: "Visit a Princess Auto Ltd. location for a solution if the tool does not function properly or parts are missing. If unable to do so, have a qualified technician service the tool."
- Script wording (stronger than before): "Princess Auto's product page and the drill's own manual list no warranty length. The store's ROYAL SERVICE Promise names no time limit and does not use the word lifetime."

**GF-W4. Princess Auto returns text re-captured today (tier a, G4).** This replaces the 2026-09-08 capture in §10.
- https://www.princessauto.com/en/shipping-and-returns: "If you are making a return or exchange, and have misplaced your receipt, all we need to do is look up your information and the appropriate purchase in order to process the return or exchange. We honour our ROYAL SERVICE Promise and accept returns at any of our store locations."
- No time limit appears on the page.
- The FAQ, re-captured today, repeats the promise verbatim (G5).

**GF-W5. Two more RIDGID conditions to say on air (tier a, shared North American site, G6).** Both come from register.ridgidpower.com/about_lsa, local copy tlw/rid_about.html.
- "Reconditioned tools are not eligible for the Lifetime Service Agreement or 25 Year Free Maintenance Agreement." Reconditioned tools get "a 1 Year Limited Warranty".
- "Our Lifetime Service Agreement means that any registered power tool or battery that fails with regular use, will be repaired or replaced, for free."
- **What "life" means is still not defined.** The only "usable life of the original product" wording sits in the hand-tool LIMITED LIFETIME text, not in the LSA. Keep U9 open: do not define "life".

**GF-W6. Bosch does have a hand-tool lifetime (tier a, G7).** This corrects §6 and §12 ("Hand-tool lifetime: none found").
- boschtools.com/ca/en/service/product-warranty/: "Limited Lifetime Warranty of Bosch Hand Tools… each BOSCH®-branded hand tool manufactured for sale in the United States and Canada… will be free from defects in material or workmanship for the original end-user purchaser's lifetime."
- It applies to model numbers "beginning with H". It does not cover cordless tools.

**GF-W7. Every other "lifetime" on the brands' Canadian pages was re-read. None covers a cordless drill (tier a, G8).**
- Milwaukee (milwaukeetool.ca): limited lifetime on hand tools, non-rechargeable personal lighting, the Hex-LOK 2-in-1 Handle, HOLE DOZER hole saws, the INSIDER box ratchet socket ("LIFETIME GUARANTEE"), REDSTICK levels (accuracy), and "the LED in the LED Work Light (49-24-0171) and the LED Upgrade Bulb (49-81-0090)". The page says "*This warranty does not cover battery packs or all power tools."
- DeWalt (dewalt.ca): "LIMITED LIFETIME WARRANTY" on SDS MAX and SOS Plus 2 Cutter masonry drill *bits*, a "FULL LIFETIME WARRANTY" on mechanics tools, and a limited lifetime on hand tools "(not to exceed one year for products with electrical or electronic components)". The "7 YEAR LIMITED WARRANTY" is finite. The products that carry it are still not identified.
- Makita (makita.ca warranty statement): no lifetime term.
- Craftsman, Ryobi, RIDGID: lifetime on hand tools only (§7, §2, §1).
- **Conclusion for the key claim:** see tl_gaps.md §2. Among the 10, only RIDGID's own Canadian page puts "FOR LIFE" on a cordless drill. It is conditional.

**GF-W8. Craftsman combo-kit page on craftsman.ca (tier a, G9).**
- https://www.craftsman.ca/en-ca/product/cmck200c2/v20-cordless-2-tool-combo-kit-2-15ah-batteries-and-charger. Title: "V20* Cordless 2-Tool Combo Kit With (2) 1.5ah Batteries And Charger".
- "Includes (1) CMCD700 Drill/Driver (1) CMCF800 Impact Driver (2) V20 20V MAX* Lithium Ion Batteries (1) V20 20V MAX* Lithium Ion Charger (1) Storage Bag (1) PH #2 Bit (1) Double Sided Bit". Warranty: "3 Year Limited Warranty".
- This is the model RONA's flyer item 00276545 is tied to. The tie rests on the rona.ca product URL slug, because the page itself still returns 403 today.

# TOOL BRANDS (CORDLESS DRILL) — PRICE DOSSIER

Project: Canadian Counter, "We Investigated 10 Tool Brands Sold in Canada (Only 1 Covers Your Cordless Drill for Life)". Upload 2026-10-07.
Dossier: prices only. Warranty, ownership and recall facts belong to the sister dossiers (tl_warranty.md, tl_ownership.md).

## 0. Capture stamp (read this first)

- All retailer captures were taken **2026-10-06 between 01:00 and 01:15 UTC**. In Toronto, Calgary and Vancouver local time that is the evening of **2026-10-05** (21:00 to 21:15 Eastern). The script should say "captured on the sixth of October 2026" only if the producer accepts the UTC date. Otherwise say "the evening of the fifth of October", or re-capture during the day on 6 October. Raw files are in `scratchpad/tl/`.
- Method: Canadian Tire public APIs (`apim.canadiantire.ca`, the same calls the canadiantire.ca website makes), Home Depot Canada public APIs (`homedepot.ca/api/...`), the Princess Auto site search (Constructor.io, the same call princessauto.com makes) plus product pages, and Flipp (backflipp.wishabi.com) for retailer flyers. No price below is estimated. Where a field was not available it is marked **NOT CAPTURED**.
- Prices exclude sales tax. Canadian Tire's API returns a separate `feeValue` on some items, listed in the "fee" column. It looks like a provincial environmental or battery levy, but that reading is not confirmed, so it is reported as a raw number.

Note for the script: "Milwaukee" at Canadian Tire is a different product line (hand trucks and dollies under the name "Milwaukee"). Do not show it as Milwaukee Tool.

## 3. Definitions used

- **Entry-level drill kit**: the lowest-priced cordless 18V/20V-class drill/driver kit that the retailer lists with at least 1 battery and a charger. Where the brand has no plain drill/driver kit at that retailer, the cheapest drill kit is used and flagged (Makita, Powerfist and DeWalt-at-CT alternates are hammer drill kits).
- **Basic 2-tool combo**: drill (or hammer drill) plus impact driver, 2 batteries, charger. If the cheapest combo has a hammer drill, that is flagged.
- **$ per kit** = current shelf price. **$ per Ah** = current price ÷ (number of batteries × Ah per battery), using the retailer's own stated Ah. No Ah is assumed where the retailer page does not state it.
- **Battery price**: standalone price of one 2.0 Ah battery for the platform, or the nearest capacity sold, at the same retailer. $/Ah is shown for that too.

## 9. Findings worth considering for the script (all tier a, retailer's own data; say the date and store every time)

1. **Lowest entry-kit shelf price**: Ryobi PCL201K1X at $68.00, Home Depot Gerrard Square, Toronto (1 × 1.5 Ah). On sale, Mastercraft 054-8737 is $69.99 at Canadian Tire Yonge & Church (regular $129.99).
2. **Lowest $/Ah among entry kits**: Black+Decker BDCDD120C-2 on sale at CT, $30.00/Ah. Next: Mastercraft 054-8737 on sale, $35.00/Ah; Bosch GSR18V-400B22 at HD, $42.25/Ah. Without sales: Bosch HD $42.25, Ryobi $45.33, RIDGID $48.50, Milwaukee 3601-22CT $49.50.
3. **Milwaukee 3601-21P (1 battery) and 3601-22CT (2 batteries) are both $198.00** at HD Gerrard Square on the same capture. Same price, double the included capacity.
4. **Ryobi's 2-pack of 2.0 Ah batteries ($78) is priced below its single 2.0 Ah ($88)** at HD 7080, 7037 and 7259. **Mastercraft's PWR POD 2-pack ($129.99) costs $10.01 more than two singles ($59.99 each)** at CT 150. Both are price facts, not wrongdoing; say so.
5. **Standalone 2.0 Ah battery spread**: $39.99 (Powerfist, online) to $148.00 (Milwaukee 48-11-1820, HD). That is 3.7 times.
6. **Same DeWalt kit, two regular prices**: DCK277D2 is regular $299.99 at CT and was-$319.00 at HD.
7. **Craftsman cordless drills were not found at Canadian Tire or Home Depot Canada.** They appear in the current RONA / RONA+ flyer in all three cities.
8. **Milwaukee and Makita power tools were not found at Canadian Tire store 150.**

## 11. UNVERIFIED / DO-NOT-USE

1. **Craftsman kit contents and single-drill price**: rona.ca returns a Cloudflare "Just a moment..." 403 to both the curl fetch and WebFetch. The Ah of the CMCK200C2 batteries is known only from a US craftsman.com title (S40) and **must not be used as Canadian**. RONA's single drill/driver kit price was not captured.
2. **Bosch GSR18V-400B2 (CT) and GXL18V-27B22 (CT) battery Ah**: the CT pages do not state it. Do not borrow HD's GSR18V-400B22 contents; the model suffix differs.
3. **Maximum combo 054-3196 battery count**: CT's spec and feature bullet conflict (2 vs "2Ah battery").
4. **Milwaukee 2697-22CT battery Ah**: HD's title and spec say 1.5 Ah, but HD's includes list names 48-11-1820 (2.0 Ah). Do not quote $/Ah for this kit.
5. **Maximum 054-3203**: one CT page says two 2Ah, its own page says one 2.0Ah. Not used.
6. **HD sale end dates**: the HD price API returns no end date. All HD "was" prices (R97801, DCK277D2, DCK225D2, 2697-22CT, LXT211S, BD2KITCDDI, DCB203-2, BL1830B) need a screenshot of the product page showing any "offer ends" text.
7. **HD `productStatus` codes** (LA, SS, X, OA, LC) and CT `feeValue`: meaning not published by the retailer. Do not interpret them on screen.
8. **CT 499-4235 Maximum $99.98 at store 150 vs $199.99 elsewhere**: the API gives no sale flag or reason. Do not call it clearance, an error or a markdown without a shelf photo.
9. **Princess Auto city prices**: not testable through the site search; PA gives one online price. There is no PA store near CT 150 on record here.
10. **Princess Auto unbranded "20V Cordless 2-Tool Kit" ($139.99) and "3-Tool Combo Kit" ($179.99)**: brand not on the page; do not call them Powerfist.
11. **Hyper Tough (Walmart)**: the Flipp e-commerce listing has no store, date or SKU. Do not use without a Walmart.ca product page capture.
12. **CT `vendorName` / `vendorNumber` fields** are present in the CT product-family JSON for house-brand items. Under house rules they were **deliberately not read into this dossier**. Never use them to name a maker of Mastercraft, Maximum or PWR POD.
13. **Incidental warranty strings** in CT/HD data (e.g. CT 054-8737 "3 year exchange warranty", 054-1349 "1 yr tool repair; 3 yr battery/charger repair", RIDGID page "Lifetime Service Agreement" text, Milwaukee page warranty text) belong to tl_warranty.md and are not verified here.
14. **Product marketing claims** in retailer descriptions (torque, runtime, "more power", "durability") are performance claims and must not be read into the script.
15. **Recalls / safety notices**: none were looked up for this dossier. Princess Auto's footer links a "Notice & Recalls" page that was not opened. Nothing goes in the script.
16. **Capture date**: UTC 2026-10-06 = local evening 2026-10-05 (section 0).
17. **Sale timing vs upload (2026-10-07)**: the Mastercraft combo sale ends 2026-10-08 (Toronto, Vancouver) and 2026-10-07 (Calgary), and the CT flyer runs to 2026-10-09. The Mastercraft drill sale ends 10-14/10-15 and the RONA Craftsman flyer ends 10-14. Viewers will often see regular prices. Quote the regular price next to every sale price.

- Arithmetic: $129.00 ÷ (2 × 1.5 Ah) = **$43.00 per Ah** on sale; $199.00 ÷ 3.0 Ah = $66.33 per Ah regular (flyer "was" $199 as captured in §5).
