# DOSSIER: BATTERY PLATFORM AND FINE PRINT, 10 TOOL BRANDS SOLD IN CANADA

Project: The Canadian Counter, "We Investigated 10 Tool Brands Sold in Canada (Only 1 Covers Your Cordless Drill for Life)", uploading 2026-10-07.
Scope: (a) "20V MAX" vs 18V disclosures; (b) battery-compatibility and tool-count claims; (c) warranty clauses about non-genuine parts, batteries or unauthorized sellers; (d) replacement-battery price vs a kit that contains the same battery.
**All captures were made on 2026-10-06** unless a line says otherwise. Local copies are in `scratchpad/tlplat/` (HTML, JSON, manuals) and, for pages first captured by the warranty dossier the same day, `scratchpad/tlw/`.
Tiers: (a) primary (brand's own Canadian page, or the Canadian retailer's own product/policy page or manual); (b) named outlet; (c) do-not-use; (d) survey/aggregator.
Rules applied: every quote is verbatim; no durability, quality or performance claim is stated as fact (brand marketing lines that make such claims are flagged and kept out); no manufacturer or supplier is named for Mastercraft, MAXIMUM or Powerfist; safety warnings and recalls are in the DO-NOT-USE list only.
Coordination: no prices dossier (`tl_prices.md`) existed in the scratchpad when this was written, so section D captures its own examples. If the prices dossier lands with different numbers for the same SKUs and store, use the later capture and note the time.

---

## 0. BOTTOM LINE: THE SIX FINE-PRINT FACTS MOST LIKELY TO SURPRISE A VIEWER

1. **"20V MAX" is 18 volts nominal, and five of the ten-plus brands say so in their own footnotes.** DeWalt, Craftsman and Black+Decker Canada use the same sentence: "*Maximum initial battery voltage (measured without a workload) is 20 volts. Nominal voltage is 18." Canadian Tire's own PWR POD FAQ says: "The batteries are 20V MAX, equaling to 18V nominal voltage." The Mastercraft drill manual says: "*Maximum battery voltage without workload; with workload nominal voltage is 18 V." (Sections A1–A5.)
2. **A MAXIMUM "20V" product lists 18V in its own specs.** The manual for the MAXIMUM 20V brushless hammer drill and impact driver kit (054-7970-0) lists the motor as "18V DC brushless". Canadian Tire's spec table for the MAXIMUM 20V 2.0Ah battery (0546702P) reads "Battery Voltage (V) = 18 V". (A6.)
3. **Every platform is closed to other brands, and Canadian Tire says so in writing.** "The batteries cannot be used with other 20V products from other brands" (PWR POD FAQ). Bosch is the one exception among the brands checked: its Canadian page says its 18V batteries work with "a growing number of cordless tools from other leading professional brands" through AMPShare. (B9, B5.)
4. **Ryobi's Canadian page dates its compatibility promise to 1996.** "The future of RYOBI is built on the same foundation we've held since 1996 – ONE+ battery compatibility". (B1.)
5. **Milwaukee's Canadian site gives three different M18 counts.** Its M18 page shows "250+ Tools" and, lower on the same page, "over 200 performance-driven solutions". An image description on its charger page reads "ONE SYSTEM, 300+ TOOLS." (B3.)
6. **The kit can cost less than the battery that comes in it.** At Home Depot Canada, Gerrard Square, Toronto, on 2026-10-06, the RIDGID R86001K drill kit cost $97. RIDGID's AC9302 battery-and-charger kit, which contains the same R87002 2.0 Ah battery and R86093 charger with no drill, cost $178. (D2.) Ryobi, Milwaukee and Mastercraft show the same pattern (D1, D4, D5).

**Warranty-voiding wording (C):** only **Makita** has a clause that names non-genuine *batteries*: "the use of non-Makita brand batteries will void all warranties for these products." Bosch excludes "Damage caused by use of non-genuine Bosch parts". The Mastercraft and MAXIMUM manuals exclude products where "any part from another manufacturer is installed therein". DeWalt and Milwaukee tie coverage to authorized sellers. **No clause naming non-genuine batteries was found for Ryobi, RIDGID, Milwaukee, DeWalt, Craftsman, Black+Decker or Powerfist** on the pages checked.

---

## A. "20V MAX" VS 18V: WHAT EACH BRAND'S OWN CANADIAN PAGE SAYS

| # | Brand | Verbatim disclosure | Where | Src |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A1 | **DeWalt** | "*Maximum initial battery voltage (measured without a workload) is 20 volts. Nominal voltage is 18." | dewalt.ca 20V MAX platform page; same sentence in the "Disclaimers" field of DCB205 battery and DCD794D1 drill kit product pages | P1, P2 |
| A1b | DeWalt (FLEXVOLT) | "* Maximum initial battery voltage (measured without a workload) is 20, 60, and 120 volts. Nominal voltage is 18, 54, and 108. 120V MAX* is based on using 2 DEWALT 60V MAX* Lithium ion batteries combined having a maximum initial battery voltage (measured with a workload) of 120 volts and a nominal voltage of 108" | dewalt.ca FLEXVOLT system page | P3 |
| A1c | DeWalt (home page) | "*Maximum initial battery voltage (measured without a workload) is 20 and 60 volts. Nominal voltage is 18 and 54." | dewalt.ca/en-ca home | P4 |
| A2 | **Craftsman (V20)** | "*Maximum initial battery voltage (measured without a workload) is 20 volts. Nominal voltage is 18." | craftsman.ca "Disclaimers" field on CMCD700C1 V20 drill kit and CMCB202-2 V20 battery 2-pack; home page reads "*20V MAX battery, maximum initial battery voltage (measured without a workload) is 20 volts. Nominal voltage is 18." The battery kit's own contents line reads "(2) CMCB202 V20* 20V MAX 2.0Ah Li-ion Batteries" | P5, P6, P7 |
| A3 | **Black+Decker** | Home page: "*Maximum initial battery voltage (measured without a workload) is 20 volts. Nominal voltage is 18." POWERCONNECT page: "*Maximum initial battery voltage (measured without a workload) is 20 volts. The nominal voltage is 18." | blackanddecker.ca home and /solutions/powerconnect | P8, P9 |
| A4 | **Mastercraft / PWR POD (Canadian Tire)** | FAQ, under "What is the voltage of the batteries?": "The batteries are 20V MAX, equaling to 18V nominal voltage. Nominal voltage is the voltage of a fully charged battery when delivering rated capacity at a specific discharge rate." | canadiantire.ca PWR POD FAQ | P10 |
| A4b | Mastercraft (Canadian Tire brand page) | "**Maximum battery voltage without workload; with workload normal voltage is 18V." (The page says "normal", not "nominal". Quote it exactly.) | canadiantire.ca/en/all-brands/pwr-pod/mastercraft.html | P11 |
| A4c | Mastercraft (owner's manual, 20V brushless 2-speed drill/driver kit 0548703P) | "*Maximum battery voltage without workload; with workload nominal voltage is 18 V." | Manual PDF linked from the Canadian Tire product page | P12 |
| A4d | Mastercraft product listings | Listings use "20V max*" with an asterisk (e.g. 0547546P: "two 20V max* (2.0 Ah) lithium-ion batteries"). No footnote explaining the asterisk was found in the product-page data itself. The explanation sits on the FAQ and brand pages above | Canadian Tire product API, store 150 | P13 |
| A5 | **Bosch** | Bosch markets its platform as "18V", for example "Compatible with all Bosch 18V lithium-ion batteries". No "20V MAX"-style footnote was found or needed. The site menus also list "12V Max Cordless Power Tools"; **no explanatory footnote for "12V Max" was found** on the 12V category page checked | boschtools.com/ca/en core18v and 12V category pages | P14, P15 |
| A6 | **MAXIMUM (Canadian Tire)** | No nominal-voltage footnote was found on the MAXIMUM brand page. But the 054-7970-0 hammer-drill kit manual lists "MOTOR 18V DC brushless", and Canadian Tire's spec table for 0546702P ("MAXIMUM 20V 2.0Ah Max Li-Ion Battery Pack") reads "Battery Voltage (V) = 18 V". The 4.0Ah 2-pack (0544266P) spec reads "Battery Voltage (V) = 20 V". The two specs for the same platform are not consistent | Manual PDF; CT product API | P16, P17, P18 |
| A7 | **Powerfist (Princess Auto)** | Product pages list "Voltage Rating (V DC) 20". No nominal-voltage disclosure was found. No owner's manual was linked; only safety data sheets were, and those were not used | princessauto.com product pages | P19 |
| A8 | Ryobi, RIDGID, Milwaukee, Makita | These brands market "18V" (Ryobi "18V ONE+", RIDGID "18V", Milwaukee "M18", Makita "18V LXT"). No 20V MAX claim, so no disclosure applies | brand pages | P20–P23 |

**Script-safe framing (suggested):** "Five of these brands sell '20V MAX' batteries. Their own fine print says the nominal voltage is 18. The 18-volt brands on this list just call it 18." Do not say or imply that 20V MAX tools perform the same as, better than or worse than 18V tools. That would be a performance claim.

## B. BATTERY COMPATIBILITY AND TOOL-COUNT CLAIMS (verbatim, brand's own Canadian page)

**B1. Ryobi 18V ONE+** (ryobitools.ca/pages/one-plus, P20)
- "With 300+ tools powered by one RYOBI 18V ONE+ lithium battery, tackle any task - Home Improvement, lawn maintenance, cleaning and beyond. Hundreds of Tools, Endless Possibilities."
- "Travel back in time to see how RYOBI has never left customers behind with a battery platform that is compatible across products from the last 3 decades."
- "The future of RYOBI is built on the same foundation we've held since 1996 – ONE+ battery compatibility, unmatched breadth of tools, and a promise to our users."
- Timeline entry, 1996: "RYOBI debuts its first cordless drill powered by a Ni-Cd battery – laying the foundation for a long lasting, compatible battery platform."
- Timeline entry, 2007: "…while remaining fully compatible with the ONE+ platform. Today, the system now includes over 300+ compatible products." (sic, "over 300+")
- Battery product page, PBP2108 8Ah 2-pack (P24): "Part of the RYOBI ONE+ system – any 18V ONE+ battery works with any 18V ONE+ product."
- Note: the brief's wording "compatible with all ONE+ tools since 1996" does **not** appear verbatim. Use the two lines above.
- Page footer: "Only At The Home Depot".

**B2. RIDGID 18V** (ca.powertools.ridgid.com, P21, P25)
- 18V collection page: "RIDGID® offers best-in-class 18V tools that are compatible with any generation of our 18V batteries. All these tools are backed by the industry-leading Lifetime Service Agreement. When you register your tools within 90 days, the Lifetime Service Agreement gives you free parts, free service, and free batteries for life." ("best-in-class" and "industry-leading" are RIDGID's marketing words. Attribute them; do not adopt them.)
- Battery product pages: AC8400802 2.0 Ah: "this 2.0 Ah battery is 100% compatible with all RIDGID 18V tools and Lithium-ion chargers." AC840060 6.0 Ah: "As always, this RIDGID 6.0 Ah MAX Output battery is 100% compatible with all RIDGID 18V tools." AC86096 charger: "As always, this RIDGID 18V 6-Port Sequential Charger is 100% compatible with all RIDGID 18V batteries."
- No tool count was found on the Canadian RIDGID pages checked.

**B3. Milwaukee M18** (milwaukeetool.ca, P22, P26, P27)
- M18 page, counter graphic: "1 system" / "250+ Tools".
- Same page, lower: "We are committed to protecting our users's investments by providing high performing, trade-focused solutions that are fully compatible with M18™ REDLITHIUM™ batteries. The M18™ system has over 200 performance-driven solutions, and growing!" (sic, "users's")
- M18 Batteries & Chargers FAQ: "All M18™ Batteries are compatible with M18™ tools & chargers." and "All M18™ batteries fit M18™ tools."
- M18/M12 Four Bay charger page (48-59-1818), image alt text: "The image highlights the compatibility of M18 batteries with over 300 tools. Text reads, "FITS M18 TOOLS" and "ONE SYSTEM, 300+ TOOLS."" This is text inside a product image as described by the page's own alt text. Quote it as "an image on Milwaukee Canada's charger page reads…".
- **Fine-print fact:** Milwaukee Canada's own pages give 200+, 250+ and 300+ for the same M18 system. Present all three without suggesting why they differ.

**B4. DeWalt 20V MAX / FLEXVOLT** (dewalt.ca, P1, P3, P28)
- 20V MAX page: "With 300+ products that keep you productive on the jobsite, 20V MAX* is a system you can build on."
- FLEXVOLT page: "With patented technology that automatically switches voltage when you change your tools, 20V/60V MAX* FLEXVOLT ® Batteries power 250+ products across the 20V MAX* and 60V MAX* systems."
- FLEXVOLT page: "FLEXVOLT ® batteries are compatible with DEWALT 20V MAX* power tools."
- Systems page (P28), POWERSTACK blurb: "They're compatible with the full 18V XR DEWALT range of tools and chargers, for a whole new kind of portable power." This appears on dewalt.ca/en-ca. "18V XR" is not the name used for the platform elsewhere on the Canadian site. See DO-NOT-USE 6.
- No one-way limit statement (for example, 20V MAX batteries in 60V tools) was found on the Canadian pages checked. Do not state one.

**B5. Bosch 18V** (boschtools.com/ca/en, P14, P29, P30)
- 18V chargers block: "Compatible with all Bosch 18V lithium-ion batteries"
- EXPERT batteries FAQ: "The new EXPERT 18V High Power batteries are forward and backward compatible with Bosch 18V cordless power tools and chargers, allowing you to upgrade your power source without needing to replace your tools."
- Same FAQ: "AMPShare™ is a multi-brand battery system that allows you to use your Bosch 18V batteries with a growing number of cordless tools from other leading professional brands."
- GBA18V40 battery page: "Compatibility – engineered for 18V and compatible with Bosch 18V tools and chargers" / "AMPShare™ System compatibility – 18V batteries and chargers are compatible with Bosch 18V power tools and a growing list of cordless products from AMPShare™ brands"
- No tool count was found. The other AMPShare brands are not named on the pages captured; do not name them.

**B6. Makita 18V LXT** (makita.ca, P23)
- "Today, the LXT System is the world's largest compatible 18V slide-style battery system with over 350 solutions."
- "ULTIMATE COMPATIBILITY / 350+ PRODUCTS. ONE SYSTEM."
- "The LXT System is the world's largest compatible cordless tool system powered by 18V slide-style batteries."
- "Makita was the first to introduce 18V lithium-ion cordless tools."
- "By using two 18V LXT Batteries, you get 36V of power and all your cordless tools operate within one system."
- "World's largest" and "first" are Makita's claims. Attribute them as "Makita says"; the channel has not verified them.

**B7. Craftsman V20** (craftsman.ca, P5, P6)
- CMCB202-2 battery page: "They are compatible with all the CRAFTSMAN® V20* cordless power tool and outdoor tool lineup."
- CMCD700C1 drill kit page heading: "V20* system compatible"
- No tool count was found.

**B8. Black+Decker 20V MAX / POWERCONNECT** (blackanddecker.ca, P8, P9)
- "Same battery, new look–20V MAX* POWERCONNECT ™ batteries work with all the 20V MAX* BLACK+DECKER ® products you already own."
- Home page: "Plus, the batteries work with all the 20V MAX* BLACK+DECKER products you already own."

**B9. Mastercraft / PWR POD (Canadian Tire), including the platform change** (P10, P11, P13, P31)
- **Platform change, as documented:** current Mastercraft 20V kits ship with batteries branded "PWR POD", a Canadian Tire multi-brand battery. Canadian Tire's spec field for these kits reads "Battery Platform = PWR POD" (e.g. 0548703P, 0548737P).
- Backward compatibility, FAQ: "Yes, all PWR POD™ batteries are compatible with Mastercraft® 20V and Simoniz® 20V tools and chargers that are currently available." (Question: "Can I use the PWR POD™ battery with my Mastercraft® 20V or Simoniz® 20V tools that I already own?")
- Brand page: "100% compatible with Mastercraft 20V MAX** battery system" / "Power Mastercraft 20V MAX** tools with PWR POD batteries. 20V MAX** batteries and chargers also work with PWR POD compatible products."
- Batteries page: "PWR POD is 100% compatible with all Mastercraft 20V MAX tools".
- Kit listings 0547546P and 0547551P: "100% compatible with existing Mastercraft 20V MAX battery system"
- **Limit, FAQ:** "PWR POD™ batteries are compatible with Mastercraft® 20V and Simoniz® 20V products, as well as any products marked with the PWR POD™ symbol. The batteries cannot be used with other 20V products from other brands."
- Chargers, FAQ: "Only PWR POD™ and Mastercraft® 20V product line chargers can be used."
- Cross-brand within Canadian Tire, FAQ: "Never before in Canada has there been a platform that works across multiple brands! PWR POD™ currently powers compatible products within Mastercraft®, SIMONIZ®, Yardworks, Woods® and Outbound, with more brands on the way." ("Never before in Canada" is Canadian Tire's claim. Attribute it.)
- Count, Mastercraft kit listings: "The Mastercraft Drill/Driver Kit is one of over 100 products powered by PWR POD." This counts PWR POD products across all the brands, not Mastercraft alone.
- 0547546P listing: "powered by PWR POD, Canada's ultimate Universal Battery System" (Canadian Tire marketing. Attribute it.)
- PWR POD warranty, FAQ: "PWR POD™ batteries and chargers are guaranteed for two (2) years from the date of original retail purchase against defects in materials and workmanship."
- No statement giving a date for, or a reason behind, the move from Mastercraft 20V MAX batteries to PWR POD was found. Do not state when or why it changed.

**B10. MAXIMUM (Canadian Tire), XVOLT** (P16, P17, P32)
- Brand page: "MAXIMUM XVOLT: Multi-Voltage Battery Platform" / "Get reliable power when you need it most. Compatible with all MAXIMUM 40V and 20V tools." / "The all-new MAXIMUM XVOLT is an innovative multi-voltage battery platform that automatically changes voltage when you change the tool. The XVOLT battery provides power for 40V tools and extended run time for 20V tools."
- 0546702P (20V 2.0Ah): "Compatible with all MAXIMUM branded 20V Max Li-Ion cordless power tools (each sold separately)"
- 0548715P (40V 4.0Ah/20V 8.0Ah): "Delivers 4.0Ah runtime in the MAXIMUM 40V MAX platform for extended use" / "Provides 8.0Ah runtime in the MAXIMUM 20V MAX platform for high-demand tools"
- 0548798P kit: "XVOLT battery automatically switches voltage when used with any 40V MAXIMUM tool" / "XVOLT battery double the Ah when used on MAXIMUM 20V max tools for extended run time"
- 054-7970-0 manual lists the compatible batteries as "054-6702-6 (2.0 Ah); 054-7983-0 (X-VOLT) 054-3203-2 (2.0 Ah); 054-3206-6 (4.0 Ah)" and the compatible chargers as "054-7985-6; 054-3204-0; 7109-B-2".
- Do **not** compare XVOLT with DeWalt FLEXVOLT or suggest any link between them. That would be an inference about a house brand's supplier or design origin.

**B11. Powerfist (Princess Auto)** (P19, P33)
- Battery page (20V Li-ion Batteries, PA1000005531): "This Li-ion battery works with the Powerfist 20V cordless tool system. It features LED charge indicator lights."
- 2.0 Ah battery-and-charger kit (PA0009071895): "It features quick-charging capabilities and is compatible with a full range of Powerfist 20V tools."
- Hammer drill kit (PA0009054685): "The battery works with the Powerfist 20V cordless battery system."
- No count, no backward-compatibility statement and no platform history were found.

**Tool-count summary (each the brand's own figure, as worded):** Makita "over 350 solutions" / "350+ PRODUCTS"; Ryobi "300+ tools"; DeWalt 20V MAX "300+ products" (FLEXVOLT batteries: "250+ products across the 20V MAX* and 60V MAX* systems"); Milwaukee M18 "250+ Tools", "over 200 performance-driven solutions", and in an image "300+ TOOLS"; PWR POD "over 100 products" (across Canadian Tire brands). RIDGID, Bosch, Craftsman, B+D, MAXIMUM, Powerfist: no count found. These are marketing counts, and each brand counts different things ("tools", "products", "solutions"). Do not rank the brands on them.

## C. WARRANTY CLAUSES ABOUT NON-GENUINE BATTERIES, PARTS OR SELLERS (verbatim)

| # | Brand | Verbatim clause | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| C1 | **Makita** | "Regarding lithium ion products and chargers; the use of non-Makita brand batteries will void all warranties for these products." | Makita Canada Warranty Statement (P34) |
| C2 | **Bosch** | "WHAT IS NOT COVERED: (1) Damage caused by use of non-genuine Bosch parts is not covered by this Limited Warranty." (Names "parts", not batteries specifically.) | Bosch Canada product warranty page (P35) |
| C3 | **Mastercraft** (manual, 0548703P) | "g) this warranty will not apply to any product or part thereof if any part from another manufacturer is installed therein or any repairs or alterations have been made or attempted by unauthorized persons;" Also: "i) this warranty will not apply to component parts sold by and identified as the product of another company, which shall be covered under the product manufacturer's warranty, if any." | Manual PDF (P12) |
| C3b | Mastercraft (manual), commercial use | "b) this warranty will not apply to any product or part thereof which is worn or broken or which has become inoperative due to abuse, misuse, accidental damage, neglect or lack of proper installation, operation or maintenance (as outlined in the applicable owner's manual or operating instructions) or which is being used for industrial, professional, commercial or rental purposes;" | P12 |
| C4 | **MAXIMUM** (manual, 054-7970-0) | "h) this warranty will not apply to any parts other than original parts, except to the extent that the retailer or manufacturer or persons authorized by either of them have repaired or replaced them;" / "j) this warranty will not apply to any product or part thereof if any part from another manufacturer is installed therein or any repairs or alterations have been made or attempted by unauthorized persons;" / "g) … any such unauthorized repairs or attempted repairs shall void this warranty in its entirety;" | Manual PDF (P16) |
| C4b | MAXIMUM, battery service requirement | "For proper servicing, both the battery and charger will require diagnosis to evaluate warranty requirements and therefore must both be provided to the Service Provider at the time of service request." | P16 |
| C5 | **DeWalt** (seller, not battery) | "Because DEWALT cannot control the quality of products sold by unauthorized sellers, this warranty applies only if the product was purchased from DEWALT or a DEWALT authorized seller, unless otherwise prohibited by law." / "DEWALT reserves the right to reject warranty claims for products purchased from unauthorized sellers, including unauthorized internet sites." The same wording is repeated for the 90-Day Money Back Guarantee and the One-Year Free Service Contract. Also: "ALTERATION OF THE SAFETY FEATURES OF THE PRODUCT SHALL VOID THIS LIMITED WARRANTY." | DeWalt Canada warranty page (P36) |
| C6 | **Milwaukee** (seller, not battery) | "Every MILWAUKEE Battery Pack (sold with cordless product and/or as a replacement battery pack) is warranted to the original purchaser from an authorized MILWAUKEE distributor only to be free from defects in material and workmanship." Battery packs are carved out of the tool warranty: "*This warranty does not cover … Cordless Battery Packs; …" | Milwaukee Canada Registration & Warranty (P37) |
| C7 | **Black+Decker** (commercial use, not battery) | "This product is not intended for commercial use, and accordingly, such commercial use of this product will void this warranty." | blackanddecker.ca/warranty-policy (P38; captured by warranty dossier, tlw/bd_warr.html) |
| C8 | **Ryobi** | No non-genuine battery clause. Exclusions: "does not cover any malfunction, failure or defects resulting from misuse, abuse, neglect, alteration, modification or repairs by other than Authorized Service Centers." Also: "One World Technologies, Inc. makes no warranties, representations or promises as to the quality or performance of its power tools other than those specifically stated in this warranty." | Ryobi warranty PDF linked from ryobitools.ca (P39). US-origin text ("state law", Anderson SC) |
| C9 | RIDGID, Craftsman, Powerfist | **No non-genuine battery or charger clause found** on the pages captured. RIDGID's exclusions cover "misuse, abuse, alteration, or repair by anyone other than an authorized service center" (warranty dossier R4). Powerfist product pages show no warranty term (warranty dossier §10) | — |

**Related fine print (Quebec availability notices).** These are not voiding clauses, but they are documented and checkable.
- Makita (P40): "Please note that while Makita Canada Inc. ("Makita") makes reasonable efforts to have replacement parts and/or maintenance/repair information available for certain products, please note that in accordance with section 39 (3) of the Quebec Consumer Protection Act, Makita does not guarantee the availability of replacement parts, repair services or maintenance/repair information within the meaning of section 39 of the Quebec Consumer Protection Act…"
- Craftsman, "Disclaimers" field on the CMCD700C1 and CMCB202-2 pages (P5, P6): "QUEBEC DISCLOSURE: CRAFTSMAN Canada does not guarantee the availability of replacement parts, repair services or information necessary to maintain or repair products subject to and under the meaning of the Quebec Consumer Protection Act, as amended by Bill 29."
- Script handling: say that both brands publish this notice under Quebec's consumer protection law. Do not say or imply that parts are unavailable. The notice says only that availability is not guaranteed.

**Script-safe framing:** "One brand on this list, Makita, says in its own Canadian warranty that using a battery that isn't Makita's voids every warranty on that tool. Bosch says damage from non-genuine Bosch parts isn't covered. Canadian Tire's Mastercraft and MAXIMUM manuals exclude any product with a part from another manufacturer installed." Do not say or imply that third-party batteries are unsafe. That is a safety claim (see DO-NOT-USE).

## D. REPLACEMENT BATTERY VS A KIT THAT CONTAINS IT

Store prices were captured 2026-10-06 from each retailer's own price API. Home Depot Canada store 7080 is GERRARD SQUARE, 1000 Gerrard Street East, Toronto (confirmed by Home Depot's store API). Canadian Tire store 150 is Toronto Yonge & Church, 839 Yonge St. Princess Auto prices are the online price with no store selected. Prices vary by store; check the shelf.

**D1. RYOBI (Home Depot Canada, store 7080), exact battery model match**
| Item | What's in the box (retailer's words) | Price | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| PBP006, 18V ONE+ 2.0 Ah Battery (1001604125) | "Includes: (1) PBP006 18V ONE+ Lithium-Ion 2.0 Ah Battery and an operator's manual" | $88.00 (no was-price shown) | 5 in stock |
| PSK005, 18V ONE+ 2.0 Ah Battery and Charger Kit (1001628820) | "Includes: (1) PBP006 18V ONE+ Lithium-Ion 2.0 Ah Battery, (1) PCG002 18V Charger, and operator's manual" | $128.00 | 40 in stock |
| PCL430K1, 18V ONE+ Oscillating Multi-Tool Kit (1001688507) | "Includes: PCL430B (1) 18V Multi-Tool, Plunge Cut Blade, Flush Cut Blade, Sanding Pad,(3) Pieces of Sandpaper Assortment, PBP006 2.0 Ah Battery, PCG002 18V Lithium-Ion Charger, and Operator's Manual" | $98.00 | 3 in stock |
- Arithmetic: the multi-tool kit costs $98.00 − $88.00 = **$10.00 more than the battery alone**. It costs $128.00 − $98.00 = **$30.00 less than the battery-and-charger kit**, and it adds a multi-tool. Unit price: PBP006 alone is $88.00 ÷ 2.0 Ah = $44.00 per Ah.

**D2. RIDGID (Home Depot Canada, store 7080), exact battery and charger model match**
| Item | What's in the box | Price | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| AC9302, 18V 2.0 Ah Battery and Charger Kit (1001596487) | "Includes: (1) R87002 2.0 Ah Lithium-Ion Battery, (1) R86093 18V Charger, and operator's manual" | $178.00 | 10 in stock |
| R86001K, 18V 1/2-inch Drill/Driver Kit (1001592203) | "Includes: (1) R86001 18V 1/2 in. Drill/Driver, (1) R87002 18V 2.0 Ah Battery, (1) R86093 18V Charger, Driving Bit, Tool Bag, and Operator's Manual" | $97.00 | 7 in stock |
| AC8400802, 18V 2.0 Ah Battery (1001602294) | "Includes: (1) 2.0 Ah Lithium-Ion battery and operator's manual" (battery model not named) | $99.00 | 8 in stock |
- Arithmetic: the drill kit costs $178.00 − $97.00 = **$81.00 less than the battery-and-charger kit with the same R87002 battery and R86093 charger**. It also costs **$2.00 less than a single 2.0 Ah battery** (AC8400802), though that battery's model number is not stated, so call it "a 2.0 Ah RIDGID battery". Unit price: AC8400802 is $99.00 ÷ 2.0 Ah = $49.50 per Ah.
- The LSA eligibility of standalone batteries is disputed. See the warranty dossier, DO-NOT-USE 1.

**D3. DEWALT (Home Depot Canada, store 7080), exact battery model match; the kit is on sale**
| Item | What's in the box | Price | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| DCB203, 20V MAX 2.0 Ah Compact Battery (1000663561) | "This package includes (1) 2.0Ah 20V MAX battery pack (DCB203)." | $129.00 (no was-price) | 2 in stock |
| DCD799D1, 20V MAX ATOMIC Compact 1/2-inch Hammer Drill Kit (1001770010) | "This 20V Max Atomic Kit includes (1) DCD799 20V MAX Compact Hammer Drill, (1) DCB112 12V/20V Charger, (1) DCB203 2Ah Battery, and a Bag." | **$148.00, was $239.00 (save $91.00)** | 0 in stock at store 7080 |
- Arithmetic: on sale, the kit costs $148.00 − $129.00 = **$19.00 more than the DCB203 battery alone** and adds a hammer drill and a charger. At the was-price of $239.00, it costs $110.00 more. Unit price: DCB203 is $129.00 ÷ 2.0 Ah = $64.50 per Ah. **Say "on sale" whenever the $148 is used.** The sale end date was not in the price data.

**D4. MILWAUKEE (Home Depot Canada, store 7080), exact battery model match, with an availability caveat**
| Item | What's in the box | Price | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| 48-11-1820, M18 REDLITHIUM 2.0 Ah Compact Battery (1000657860) | "Includes: (1) M18 REDLITHIUM Lithium-ion 2.0 Ah 18V Compact Battery (48-11-1820)" | $148.00 | **status code "X", 0 in stock at store 7080** |
| 3601-22CT, M18 Brushless Compact 1/2 in Drill/Driver Kit (1001824335) | "Includes: (1) M18 18-Volt Lithium-Ion Brushless Cordless 1/2 in Compact Drill/Driver (3601-20), (2) M18 REDLITHIUM 2.0 CP Batteries (48-11-1820), (1) M18/ M12 Multi-Voltage Charger (48-59-1812), (1) Contractor Bag" | $198.00 (no was-price) | status "SS", 3 in stock |
- Arithmetic: two 48-11-1820 batteries bought separately cost 2 × $148.00 = $296.00. The kit with both batteries, a charger and a drill costs $198.00, **$98.00 less**. The kit price works out to $99.00 per battery before counting the drill and charger. Unit price for the single battery: $148.00 ÷ 2.0 Ah = $74.00 per Ah.
- Caveat: Home Depot's status code for the single battery is "X" and the store shows 0 units. The code's meaning is not documented on the page. Re-check that the single battery can be bought before using this example, or present it as "listed at".

**D5. MASTERCRAFT / PWR POD (Canadian Tire, store 150 Toronto Yonge & Church). Capacity matches; the kit's battery model number is not stated**
| Item | What's in the box | Price | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0547563P, PWR POD 20V MAX 2.0 Ah Battery | 1 battery | $59.99 regular; price data also shows a separate $1.30 fee field | 0 in stock at store 150 |
| 0547566P, PWR POD 20V MAX 2.0 Ah Battery & Charger Starter Kit | battery + charger | $77.99 regular (+$1.30 fee field) | 1 in stock |
| 0548737P, Mastercraft 20V Cordless Drill/Driver with 76-pc Accessory Kit and case with PWRPOD 2 Ah Battery and Charger | spec: "Battery Charger Included, Batteries Included, Bag, Multi Bit Set"; "Batteries Included Quantity = 1" | **$69.99, was $129.99, "Save 46% ($60.00)", price valid until 2026-10-15** | 4 in stock |
| 0547546P, Mastercraft 20V 1/2-in Drill & 1/4-in Impact Driver Combo Kit with 2 PWR POD 2 Ah Batteries and Charger | "Includes: cordless 1/2" (13 mm) drill/driver, ¼" (6.4 mm) impact driver, battery charger, two 20V max* (2.0 Ah) lithium-ion batteries, 2 belt clips, 2 bit holders, 4 screwdriver bits and nylon tote" | **$149.99, was $199.99, valid until 2026-10-08** | 3 in stock |
- Arithmetic: on sale, the 0548737P drill kit costs $69.99 − $59.99 = **$10.00 more than the 2.0 Ah PWR POD battery alone** and **$8.00 less than the battery-and-charger starter kit** ($77.99). At its regular $129.99 it costs $70.00 more than the battery. Two 2.0 Ah PWR POD batteries alone cost 2 × $59.99 = $119.98. The two-tool combo with two batteries and a charger costs $149.99 on sale, $30.01 more. Unit price: 0547563P is $59.99 ÷ 2.0 Ah = $30.00 per Ah (before the fee field).
- Caveat: the "fee" is a field in Canadian Tire's price data and is not labelled on the page. Do not call it an eco fee without checking the shelf or receipt. Neither kit listing names its battery's model number. Say "a PWR POD 2 Ah battery", per the titles.
- DO-NOT-USE: the sister listing 0547551P is titled "…with 2 PWR POD 2 Ah Batteries…" but a bullet on the same page says "two 20V max* (1.5 Ah) lithium-ion batteries". The listing contradicts itself; do not use it.

**D6. POWERFIST (Princess Auto, online price, no store selected). Capacities differ; illustrative only**
| Item | Price |
|---|---|
| 20V Li-ion 2.0 Ah Battery (9054693, group PA1000005531) | $39.99 |
| 20V Li-ion 2.0 Ah Battery and Charger Kit (PA0009071895): "Includes 2.0Ah battery pack and 2A charger" | $59.99 |
| 20V 1/2 in. Li-ion Cordless Hammer Drill Kit (PA0009054685): "Includes Hammer drill, battery, charger and tool bag"; "Battery Capacity (Ah) 1.5" | $109.99 |
- Not like-for-like (1.5 Ah in the kit, 2.0 Ah sold alone), so **not usable as a "kit cheaper than battery" example**. Powerfist's battery-only price per Ah is the lowest captured: $39.99 ÷ 2.0 = $20.00 per Ah. Do not imply anything about quality from this.

**Script-ready summary line (all from D1–D2, regular prices, no sale needed):** "At the Home Depot on Gerrard Street in Toronto, on October 6th, RIDGID's battery-and-charger kit was $178. A RIDGID drill kit with the same battery and the same charger, plus a drill, was $97."

## E. UNVERIFIED / DO-NOT-USE

**Safety and recalls (never in the script):**
1. Mastercraft manual (0548703P) WARNING: "To reduce the risk of electric shock or damage to the charger and battery, use only the Mastercraft® and PWR PODTM batteries and chargers listed." This is a safety instruction. DO NOT USE in the script, and do not use it to suggest that other batteries are dangerous.
2. Battery-safety pages linked from DeWalt ("/en-ca/support/battery-safety"), Milwaukee ("Safety Notices"), Ryobi ("Important Recall Information") and Princess Auto (battery SDS PDFs) were not opened. Nothing from them goes in the script.
3. Any claim that third-party or counterfeit batteries are unsafe, or have caused fires or injuries. None was researched. DO NOT USE.

**Performance and quality marketing (attribute or omit; never state as fact):**
4. Battery and tool marketing lines captured incidentally include Ryobi "up to 2X more power and 8X more runtime", Bosch "significantly more power and longer runtime", Craftsman "up to 1.5x the runtime", PWR POD "up to 3x longer runtime", Makita "unmatched power, speed, and run time", RIDGID "best-in-class" and Milwaukee "MOST POWER, FASTEST CHARGING, and LONGEST LIFE". **DO NOT USE.** House rule: no performance claims, not even attributed ones, unless they come from a named independent tester on the same model.
5. "World's largest compatible 18V … system" (Makita), "Never before in Canada…" (PWR POD), "Canada's ultimate Universal Battery System" (Canadian Tire), "industry-leading" (RIDGID), and "CANADA'S #1 SELLING TOOL BRAND" with the footnote "*Source : TraQline Canada, Total Hand+Power tools unit share report, 2019-2020" (Mastercraft page). Use only as attributed quotes, if at all. The channel has not verified any of them.

**Unverified or needs re-check:**
6. DeWalt Canada's POWERSTACK blurb: "compatible with the full 18V XR DEWALT range". "18V XR" is not the platform name used elsewhere on the Canadian site. Do not interpret it, call it an error or build a beat on it without asking DeWalt Canada.
7. Milwaukee "300+ TOOLS" appears only in image alt text on a charger page. Present it as an image on the page, not body copy.
8. Milwaukee 48-11-1820 single battery: Home Depot status "X", 0 in stock at store 7080. Confirm it can be bought before using D4.
9. DeWalt DCD799D1 kit price is a sale ($148, was $239), 0 in stock at store 7080, and the sale end date was not captured.
10. Mastercraft 0548737P and 0547546P sale prices end 2026-10-15 and 2026-10-08 respectively, so on upload day (2026-10-07) the 0547546P price has one day left. Re-check, or say "on October 6th".
11. The Canadian Tire "fee" field ($1.30 on single PWR POD batteries, $2.60 on the 2-pack, $0.55 on MAXIMUM 0546702P) is unlabelled in the data captured. Don't name it.
12. MAXIMUM voltage: the specs conflict between listings (0546702P "18 V", 0544266P "20 V"), and the manual says "18V DC brushless". Quote the specific listing or manual. Do not generalize to "MAXIMUM admits its 20V is 18V". No MAXIMUM nominal-voltage disclosure was found.
13. Bosch "12V Max": no explanatory footnote was found on the one category page checked. Do not say Bosch has none; say "we didn't find one".
14. Powerfist: no nominal-voltage disclosure, no manual and no warranty term found online. Do not say "Powerfist doesn't disclose". Say "Princess Auto's product page lists 20 V DC and no nominal figure".
15. PWR POD's relationship to the earlier Mastercraft 20V MAX battery line rests on Canadian Tire's own compatibility statements. No date or reason for the change was found. Do not state when or why it changed.
16. AMPShare partner brands are not named on the Bosch Canada pages captured. Don't name them.
17. Ryobi warranty PDF is US-origin text served by the Canadian site. Quote it as "the warranty Ryobi Canada's site links to".
18. The brief's phrase "compatible with all ONE+ tools since 1996" is not Ryobi's wording. Use only the verbatim lines in B1.
19. Craftsman retail availability: no Canadian retailer product page was captured in this dossier (RONA blocked fetches per the warranty dossier). Craftsman's place-of-purchase list names "RONA","KENT","Amazon" (warranty dossier R22). Rely on that dossier before saying where Craftsman is sold.
20. Do not compare MAXIMUM XVOLT with DeWalt FLEXVOLT, or infer any supplier or design link for any house brand. The manuals say only "Imported by Mastercraft Canada Toronto" and "Imported by MAXIMUM Canada Toronto" with "Made in China". If used at all, those lines belong in the ownership dossier, and they name no manufacturer.
21. No independent test results were collected. Nothing here supports any statement that one platform outlasts, outperforms or is better value in use than another.

# RECORD AND CONTEXT DOSSIER: 10 tool brands sold in Canada

Video: "We Investigated 10 Tool Brands Sold in Canada (Only 1 Covers Your Cordless Drill for Life)". Upload date: Oct 7, 2026.
Dossier: record. Compiled 2026-10-06. Every capture in this file is dated 2026-10-06 unless another date is given.
Tiers: (a) primary, (b) named outlet, (c) do-not-use, (d) survey. Source IDs [S#] point to the SOURCE REGISTER at the end.

House-rule notes for the script writer:
- Every label below is exact: guilty plea, finding, allegation, settlement, consent agreement, proposed class action, dismissal.
- Every U.S. item is marked **[U.S.]**. None of them involves a Canadian regulator or court.
- Recalls and safety items appear only in the UNVERIFIED / DO-NOT-USE section.
- House brands: no manufacturer or supplier is named for Mastercraft, Maximum or Powerfist anywhere in this file. None of those brands' own primary pages names one.

---

## 0. HEADLINE FINDINGS (usable, tier a unless marked)

1. **Canadian Tire / DeWalt drill / Quebec OPC.** On 6 February 2026 Canadian Tire Corporation (Société Canadian Tire ltée) **pleaded guilty** in Montreal to 74 counts under Quebec's Consumer Protection Act and must pay **$1,287,550 in fines and fees**. The OPC alleged false reference ("regular") prices. Canadian Tire admitted guilt for 5 products, one of them **"une perceuse sans fil Dewalt"** (a DeWalt cordless drill). [S1][S2]
   - The defendant was Canadian Tire, the retailer. No charge, finding or allegation was made against DeWalt or Stanley Black & Decker. The script must say this out loud.
   - The OPC release does not name the drill model.
2. **No Competition Bureau action found** against any of the 10 core brands or their Canadian parents over tool marketing (searched 2026-10-06). **No FTC "Made in USA" action found** against DeWalt, Craftsman, Milwaukee or Stanley Black & Decker. **No class action found, in Canada or the U.S., over battery-voltage naming** such as "20V MAX". [S3][S4][S5]
3. **DeWalt's own voltage footnote on dewalt.ca:** "*Maximum initial battery voltage (measured without a workload) is 20 and 60 volts. Nominal voltage is 18 and 54." This decodes a label phrase and needs no lawsuit. [S6]
4. **One Toronto address, three brands.** Milwaukee Tool Canada and RIDGID Canada (Techtronic Industries Canada Inc) list the same Canadian address, 3381 Steeles Avenue East, Suite 400, Toronto, ON M2H 3S7. Milwaukee Canada, RIDGID Canada and Ryobi Canada all list the same customer-service number, 1-800-268-4015. [S7][S8][S9]
5. **Makita built power tools in Canada from April 1980 to January 2009**, according to Makita Corporation's own history page. Makita Canada has been based in Whitby, Ontario since November 1973. [S10][S11]
6. **Service networks, counted from each brand's own Canadian locator on 2026-10-06:** Makita 96 locations; DeWalt 104 service centres; Milwaukee 91 locations that service power tools, 10 of them Milwaukee-run branches. Full method and caveats are in section 4.

---

## 1. REGULATORY AND COURT RECORD

### 1A. Canada

| # | Matter | Exact label | Who | Brand link | Date | Source / tier |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Quebec OPC prosecution, false reference prices, s. 225 b) Consumer Protection Act | **Guilty plea** to 74 counts. Fines and fees total $1,287,550. Approved by Quebec court Judge Simon Lavoie (per CBC/CP). | Canadian Tire Corporation (Société Canadian Tire ltée) | DeWalt cordless drill was one of 5 products for which CT admitted guilt. The others were Henckels and Cuisinart knife sets and Lagostina and Heritage cookware. | Plea 2026-02-06. Prices checked April to October 2021. | [S1] a (OPC, French only); [S2] b (CBC/Canadian Press) |
| 2 | Competition Bureau actions on tool marketing (any core brand) | **None found** | — | — | searched 2026-10-06 | [S3] (search; nothing to cite) |
| 3 | Canadian class actions on tool warranties or tool marketing (any core brand) | **None found** | — | — | searched 2026-10-06 | search only |
| 4 | Canadian Tire customer data breach | **Proposed class action** (Quebec: application for authorization; also KND Complex Litigation / Hammerco) | Canadian Tire | None. Privacy matter, not tools. | Breach reported Oct 2025; article 2026-07-29 | [S12] b. Off topic, see DO-NOT-USE |

Verbatim, OPC release (French; it is the only version, and the /en/ URL serves the same French text) [S1]:
- "L'Office de la protection du consommateur (OPC) annonce que la Société Canadian Tire ltée a plaidé coupable, aujourd'hui à Montréal, à des accusations portées en vertu de la Loi sur la protection du consommateur (LPC). Pour 74 des chefs d'accusation déposés par le Directeur des poursuites criminelles et pénales, la Société devra payer des amendes totalisant 1 287 550 $ avec les frais."
- "Canadian Tire a reconnu sa culpabilité pour 5 des produits faisant l'objet de l'enquête, soit des ensembles de couteaux Henckels et Cuisinart, des batteries de cuisine Lagostina et Heritage et une perceuse sans fil Dewalt."
- "L'Office a vérifié les prix de 7 produits ciblés dans les circulaires de Canadian Tire, sur son site web ainsi que dans 3 succursales de la grande région de Montréal."

Verbatim, Canadian Tire's response as quoted by CBC/The Canadian Press, Pierre Saint-Arnaud, 2026-02-07 [S2]:
- "The OPC charges relate to five products over a six-month period five years ago. Importantly, no customers were overcharged and the matter is now concluded." (attributed to "a spokesperson for Canadian Tire")
- CBC also writes that Crown prosecutor Jérôme Dussault "says the Canadian retail giant agreed to the settlement after initially pleading not guilty."

Script guidance: say "pleaded guilty" and "fines". CBC's word "settlement" may be quoted only if attributed to the prosecutor via CBC. Do not say DeWalt did anything. The OPC's subject was Canadian Tire's flyer and website "regular price".

Yardstick primaries (Canada), for the script's yardstick segment:
- Competition Act, warranties: paragraph 74.01(1)(c) prohibits "making a representation about the warranty or guarantee of a product if it is misleading or there is no reasonable prospect that it will be carried out" (Competition Bureau page, modified 2022-06-23). [S13] a
- "Made in Canada": "at least 51% of the total direct costs of producing or manufacturing the good have been incurred in Canada", "the last substantial transformation of the good occurred in Canada", and a qualifier "such as 'Made in Canada with imported parts'". "Product of Canada": "at least 98%" plus last substantial transformation in Canada (Competition Bureau page, modified 2026-06-14). [S14] a
- None of the 10 core brands makes a "Made in Canada" claim on the pages checked.

Quebec Bill 29 notices (context, tier a, quote exactly):
- Canadian Tire's Quebec page: "Please note that Canadian Tire Corporation, Limited, and its retailers do not guarantee the availability of replacement parts, repair services, or maintenance and repair information for products within the meaning of the Quebec Consumer Protection Act." [S15] a
  - This is a statutory disclosure required in Quebec. It is not an admission or a finding, and the script must not imply one. The same page links a Stanley Black & Decker disclosure PDF covering "DeWalt, Craftsman, Stanley, Black & Decker, Irwin, Bostitch". That PDF was not opened.
- Makita Canada's footer links "Quebec Notice - Warranty of Availability (Bill 29)" (makita.ca/index2new.php?event=bill29). Not opened. [S11]

| 6 | St. Onge v. Stanley Black & Decker, Inc., D. Conn. No. 3:23-cv-00516, filed 2023-04-25 | **Proposed class action**. Allegations only. | Stanley Black & Decker / **DeWalt** | Wet/dry vacuums, horsepower claims (complaint calls them "a farce", per classaction.org) | **Current status not verified** | [S18] b, see UNVERIFIED |
| 7 | Sears / Craftsman "Made in USA" consumer-fraud suit, California state court | Class certification **denied** 2012-11-06 (judge: class would be "a procedural management nightmare," per Law360) | **Sears Roebuck**, which owned Craftsman then (SBD bought Craftsman in 2017) | Craftsman tools | No finding against Sears on the merits was located | [S19] b (Law360, Matthew Heller, 2012-11-06) |
| 8 | FTC "Made in USA" enforcement against DeWalt, Craftsman, Milwaukee or SBD | **None found** | — | — | searched 2026-10-06 | [S4] (search) |
| 9 | Battery-voltage ("20V MAX" vs 18V nominal) suits against any core brand | **None found** | — | — | searched 2026-10-06 | [S5] (search) |

Guidance: items 5 to 7 concern **wet/dry vacuums and a pre-2017 owner**, not cordless drills, and none is Canadian. If used at all, use item 5 only, as "a U.S. court dismissed a proposed class action". Better still, leave them out. The video is about drills.

## 2. CANADIAN CONNECTIONS (each with a primary source)

### Mastercraft and Maximum: Canadian Tire Corporation (Toronto)
- CTC "was incorporated under the laws of Ontario by letters patent dated December 1, 1927". "The head and registered office of CTC is located at 2180 Yonge Street, Toronto, Ontario". (2025 AIF, dated 2026-02-18) [S20] a
- "Starting from a single garage established in 1922 by brothers A.J. and J.W. Billes". [S20] a
- **Mastercraft is an owned brand:** "Among the well-known Owned Brands carried at Canadian Tire are Mastercraft, MotoMaster, Noma, Paderno, Sherwood, Simoniz and Yardworks." [S20] a. CTC corporate site: "Built on the foundation of legacy brands such as Mastercraft® and Motomaster®, our Consumer Brands Division..." [S21] a
- **Maximum:** the canadiantire.ca brand page says "Shop MAXIMUM Branded Tools to help you tackle any job. Exclusive to Canadian Tire." [S22] a. **"Exclusive to" is not "owned by".** The 2025 AIF and the 2025 Report to Shareholders do not name Maximum (checked by text search). Ownership rests on Marketing Magazine, 2016-03-15, Chris Powell: "Maximum Tools is a private label brand" with "more than 850 SKUs since its 2014 debut". [S23] b. Script wording: "Maximum, a Canadian Tire private-label brand, according to Marketing Magazine", or "sold only at Canadian Tire".
- Store network: "a network of 502 stores across the country operated by Dealers". [S20] a
- Sourcing (company-wide, not brand-specific): "In 2025, approximately 50 percent of Canadian Tire Retail ... inventory purchases were sourced directly from vendors outside Canada, primarily from Asia". [S20] a. **This must not be applied to Mastercraft or Maximum specifically, and no supplier may be named.**

### Powerfist: Princess Auto (Winnipeg)
- "Princess Auto is a private, Canadian-owned company employing over 3,600 Team Members, with our Home Office in Winnipeg, Manitoba." and "55 plus stores coast-to-coast". [S24] a
- "Princess Auto Wrecking (PAW) began life in 1933. The original owner sold the company to Harvey Tallman in 1942". [S24] a
- "In 1977, Princess Auto became a retailer, with the first store opening in Edmonton, Alberta." [S24] a
- "Bob's sons Matthew and Marc Tallman taking ownership of the business in 2018." [S24] a
- Historical Canadian manufacturing: "a small manufacturing division called Shur-Lift was created. In time, Shur-Lift Industries would become a major supplier to PAW". This is Princess Auto's history, with no date for current status. **Do not connect Shur-Lift to Powerfist.** [S24] a
- Media-room fast facts: "CEO: Ken Larson", "Founded: 1933", "Features: Exclusive provider of Powerfist and Pro.Point brand name products." [S25] a. The media room's "50 plus stores" is older than the our-story page's "55 plus".
- Promise: "No sale is final until you're satisfied". [S24][S25] a

### DeWalt, Craftsman, Black+Decker: Stanley Black & Decker Canada (Mississauga)
- dewalt.ca and craftsman.ca footers: "6275 Millcreek Drive, Mississauga, ON L5N 7K6". [S6][S26] a
- History: Black & Decker "expanded into Brockville in 1957"; "The Brockville factory became the national headquarters for the Canadian branch of Black & Decker"; "By 1972 there were 635 employees; in 1980, that number expanded to over 1,100"; "In 1998, however, the Brockville plant lost its manufacturing business and approximately 300 jobs"; "It closed in 2012." (Brockville Museum) [S27] b
- No current Canadian manufacturing by SBD power-tool brands was found.

### Milwaukee, Ryobi, RIDGID: Techtronic Industries (Hong Kong) and TTI Canada (Toronto)
- Milwaukee Tool Canada: "3381 Steeles Avenue East. Suite 400 Toronto, ON M2H 3S7"; "Canada: 1-800-268-4015". [S7] a
- RIDGID Canada contact page: "Techtronic Industries Canada Inc | 3381 Steeles Ave E Suite 400 | Toronto, ON M2H 3S7", phone 1-800-268-4015, "Only At The Home Depot". [S8] a
- Ryobi Canada: "'RYOBI' is a trademark of RYOBI Limited and is used by TTI Consumer Power Tools, Inc. pursuant to a license granted by RYOBI Limited." Customer Service 1-800-268-4015; "Only At The Home Depot". [S9] a
- TTI 2025 Annual Report: "All trademarks listed other than AEG and RYOBI are owned by the Group." "RYOBI is a registered trademark of Ryobi Limited, and is used under license." TTI is listed on "The Stock Exchange of Hong Kong Limited ... (stock code: 669)". [S28] a
- Milwaukee U.S. manufacturing **[U.S.]**: Milwaukee opened a West Bend, Wisconsin hand-tool plant (release dated 2022-08-10): a $55 million, 95,000 sq ft facility for pliers and screwdrivers. Quote: "Wisconsin has been our company's home for nearly 100 years" (Tim Albrecht, President of Hand Tools, Storage & PPE). [S29] a (company release via PR Newswire). No Canadian manufacturing found.

### Makita Canada (Whitby)
- Makita Corporation history: "Nov. 1973 Established Makita Power Tools Canada Ltd. in Canada. (Changed the name to Makita Canada Inc. in January 1991.)" and "**Apr. 1980 Started production of power tools in Canada. (Production terminated in January 2009.)**" [S10] a
- makita.ca: "We have been in Canada since 1973 employing hundreds of people during our 40+ year history. We supply and service our customers from distribution centres in Ontario and British Columbia as well as Factory Service Centres strategically located across Canada." [S11] a
- Footer: "Makita Canada Inc ... 1950 Forbes Street, Whitby, ON. L1N 7B7". [S11] a
- Makita Corporation's plant list: "We have plants in eight countries around the world (Japan, China, Romania, Thailand, the United Kingdom, Brazil, the United States, and Germany)" and "about 90% of products manufactured by Makita are made abroad". [S30] a. **Canada is not on the list.** A makita.ca page says otherwise (see DO-NOT-USE).

### Bosch Canada (Mississauga)
- Power-tools legal entity: "Robert Bosch Inc.", "6955 Creditview Rd, Mississauga, ON L5N 8E9 Canada". [S31] a
- bosch.ca: "Bosch first established its presence in Canada in 1911 during the early days of our automotive footprint expansion. The first office was opened in Montreal in 1954." Robert Bosch Inc. includes "Power Tools" among its units. [S32] a
- No Canadian power-tool manufacturing found.

## 3. COUNTRY-OF-MANUFACTURE STATEMENTS BY THE BRANDS (exact attribution; all **[U.S.]** marketing)

- **DeWalt** (dewalt.com, U.S. site): "Select products are made in the USA with global materials". "We source raw materials and components from around the world when they are either not available in the United States or not viable to purchase based on cost, quality, or availability." [S33] a
  - The same program page on dewalt.ca returned 404. The same hole saw (DAH180032) carries "Made in the usa with global materials" on dewalt.com but not on dewalt.ca (checked 2026-10-06). **On the pages checked, this is U.S. marketing, not Canadian marketing.** [S33][S34]
- **Craftsman** (craftsman.com, U.S.): the TX Workstation page says "Proudly manufactured in the United States with global materials". [S35] a. This product is storage, not a drill.
- **Craftsman Fort Worth plant [U.S.]**: SBD release, 2019-05-15: "Stanley Black & Decker (NYSE: SWK) today announced it will expand its U.S. manufacturing footprint with a new CRAFTSMAN manufacturing plant in Fort Worth, TX." Quote from Jim Loree, then President and CEO: "When we purchased CRAFTSMAN in 2017 we were determined to revitalize this iconic U.S. brand and bring back its American manufacturing heritage." Products: sockets, ratchets, wrenches, general sets. [S36] a (company release via PR Newswire). **This is a 2019 announcement. Current output and its Canadian availability are not verified.**
- **Milwaukee [U.S.]**: West Bend plant for pliers and screwdrivers (2022 release). [S29]. That release contains no "Made in USA" label wording.
- **Makita**: the corporate plant list excludes Canada. Canadian production ended January 2009. [S10][S30]
- **Mastercraft, Maximum, Powerfist**: no country-of-manufacture statement on the brands' own pages checked. **Nothing to say.**
- FTC standard, for the yardstick only, **[U.S.]**: the FTC Made in USA business page points to the Labeling Rule (16 CFR 323). The "all or virtually all" wording was not captured verbatim (see UNVERIFIED).

Method: each count comes from the data feed behind the brand's own Canadian locator page, the same data the page shows. **Film a screen capture of each locator before quoting.** Counts change daily.

By province: QC 40, ON 23, BC 11, AB 7, NB 4, MB 3, NS 3, NL 2, SK 2, PE 1 | a. **Conflict:** makita.ca history text says "a network of over 230 Authorized Service Centres". Captured via the site's own /api/store-locator with "purpose: service-center". Location-specific searches return smaller radius subsets (Vancouver 19, Halifax 42, Montreal 79). [S38] |
| **Milwaukee** | service.milwaukeetool.ca/support/find-service-center | **91 locations that service "Power Tools"** | 10 Milwaukee "Service Branch" (factory) locations + 81 authorized. (Winnipeg), Home Brite Hardware (Belleville) | a. ## UNVERIFIED / DO-NOT-USE

### Recalls and safety items (DO-NOT-USE in script under house rules)
1. Ryobi 40V push-mower recall (about 217,500 U.S. units and 28,400 in Canada, per Top Class Actions) and the related **proposed class action** Lilly v. Ryobi Technologies, Inc., E.D. Pa. No. 2:25-cv-00939, filed 2025-02-21. Recall-linked. (c)
2. Kobalt 24V/48V yard-tool recall (CPSC, 2026-07-09) and the **proposed class action** Yu v. Lowe's Companies Inc. and Greenworks North America LLC, filed 2026-07-15. Recall-linked. Kobalt is an alternate. (c)
3. Milwaukee M18 FUEL top-handle chainsaw recall and the related **proposed class action** reported by BizTimes. Recall-linked. (c)
4. SKIL PWRCore 40V battery class action against Chervon (Chervon Canada, Mississauga). Recall-linked, and not a core brand. (c)
5. The "Important Recall Information" links on Ryobi and RIDGID Canada pages, and "Safety Recall" on makita.ca. Not opened; do not use. (c)
6. Health Canada recall lists for the 10 brands were **not searched exhaustively**. Nothing in this file is a complete recall record.

### Unverified or conflicting (do not state as fact)
7. **Makita "plants ... including Canada":** makita.ca says Makita manufactures "at 10 plants operating in 8 countries, including Canada" and "a facility in the Canada". Makita Corporation's plant list names eight countries **without Canada**, and its history says Canadian production "terminated in January 2009". **Do not say Makita makes tools in Canada today.** (c)
8. **Makita "over 230 Authorized Service Centres"** (makita.ca history page) vs **96** locator entries on the same date. Attribute either figure; do not present both as fact. Factory Service Centres: 7 on the contact page vs 6 in the locator.
9. **Milwaukee count depends on method:** 91 power-tool locations (v2 sites feed, the one the current page uses) vs 101 (older serviceCenters feed). Film the page.
10. **DeWalt "104"** came from the site's locator data with no location filter. Not confirmed that the visible page ever shows all 104 at once. Film a search.
11. **St. Onge v. Stanley Black & Decker** (DeWalt vacuums, filed 2023-04-25): current status unknown (CourtListener rate-limited). Treat as **allegations in a proposed class action** only.
12. **Maximum ownership** has a tier b source only (Marketing Magazine 2016). No CTC filing or primary page names Maximum as owned. A CIPO trademark record was not retrieved.
13. **Bosch "moved to Mississauga in 1974" and "27 locations in Canada":** from search summaries, not found on bosch.ca. Do not use. Postal code also differs: imprint page L5N 8E9; bosch.ca corporate-information page L5N 1R1 (via fetch summary).
14. **Ryobi and Bosch Canadian service-centre counts:** not captured.
15. **Craftsman sold in Canada:** craftsman.ca exists and lists 16 retailers. CT and Home Depot Canada searches returned no Craftsman power tools. RONA's site blocked access (HTTP 403). The retail dossier must confirm before Craftsman is called "sold in Canada".
16. **Craftsman Fort Worth plant output in 2026, and whether any USA-made Craftsman reaches Canadian shelves:** not verified. Search summaries mention about 500 employees "as of 2023". Not a primary source; do not use.
17. **SBD New Britain plant closure (2026)** and quotes attributed to SBD such as "a structural decline in demand for single-sided tape measures": second-hand via ToolGuyd. No primary captured; U.S.; off topic. (c)
18. **Black & Decker Brockville:** another source (blog) says 500 jobs were lost in January 1998. The Brockville Museum says "approximately 300 jobs". Use the museum figure only, attributed.
19. **FTC "all or virtually all" standard:** not captured verbatim from ftc.gov. If used, quote 16 CFR 323 directly and label it U.S.
20. **DeWalt Canada image alt text** describing a compressor with "a 'Made in the USA' label". Alt text only; the product page returned 404. Do not use.
21. **Canadian Tire data-breach proposed class action:** privacy, not tools. Off topic. Do not use. (c)
22. **Shop-Vac / Lowe's "peak horsepower" settlement (2016)** and the Ontario lithium-ion battery cell price-fixing class action: not core brands and not tool-brand marketing. Do not use. (c)
23. **Milwaukee "18 USA facilities" / "$1.8 billion" and "Makita 60% Jiangsu"**: search-engine summaries of third-party sites. Do not use. (c)
24. The RIDGID LSA FAQ placeholder text ("we ship to most countries worldwide"). Template text; do not quote. (c)
25. Voltage disclaimers for Mastercraft, Maximum and Powerfist "20V" products were **not checked**. The product dossier should check them before any voltage comparison.

# DOSSIER (retention): structure for "We Investigated 10 Tool Brands Sold in Canada (Only 1 Covers Your Cordless Drill for Life)"

For: The Canadian Counter. Faceless narration of about 22 minutes, uploading **Oct 7, 2026**. Thumbnail: "ONLY 1 FOR LIFE".
Compiled: **Oct 6, 2026** (UTC capture clock read 2026-10-06T01:17Z). Every YouTube source below was pulled on Oct 6, 2026.
House blueprint adapted: `scratchpad/ct_retention.md` (Canadian Tire, Oct 4).

Raw captures (all in `scratchpad/tl/`):
- Transcripts: `tl/tr_Vx8ynE5P5Kw.txt` (British Truth Report) and `tl/tr_RwBRqb6mfd4.txt` (Australia Food Exposed). The Oz Grocery Alert transcript (494g4fZLSh8) was read in full in the tool output. Only its intro is saved, at `tl/ret/oz_intro.txt`, so its positions below are **estimates** (see U3).
- Draft lines and their measurements: `tl/ret/drafts.txt`.
- **Pre-flight checker for a 10-item script: `tl/tl_ret_check.py`.** This is `ct/ret_check.py` with "Number ten." added to the item sequence (see §8.13).

**How to read this file**
- **This dossier is about structure, not facts.** Competitor transcripts, comments and view counts are **tier (c) for any factual use**. They are used here only as structural samples. No competitor sentence may be repeated on air (§6.2 lists their template phrases).
- **Where the facts in the example lines come from.** Every warranty fact comes from the sibling dossier `scratchpad/tl_warranty.md` (source IDs [R#]). Every ownership fact comes from `scratchpad/tl_ownership.md` (source IDs [S#]). I did **not** re-open those sources. The writer checks each line against them (U7).
- **OUR NOTE** marks our own arithmetic or judgement.
- **No retention data exists for any video here** (U1). "Hit" means views. Every link to retention is inferred from structure.

## 0. VERDICT FOR THE WRITER (the 12 things that matter most)

1. **Rank on one stated yardstick, and say it in the first minute.** Use: "how long the brand's own Canadian page, or the store's listing where there's no brand page, covers a cordless drill in writing". Count batteries separately. Break ties with extras (free service, money-back, exchange window), then conditions (registration, receipt). Every item then has a number the viewer can check.
2. **Recommended order, 10 to 1:** Powerfist · Mastercraft · Makita · Craftsman · Ryobi · DeWalt · MAXIMUM · *ask* · Milwaukee · Bosch · **RIDGID**. The reasons are in §7.
3. **#1 pays the title.** RIDGID's Lifetime Service Agreement is the only lifetime coverage on cordless tools that tl_warranty found among the ten. Always say it with its condition: register within 90 days and upload the receipt. Never call it a "lifetime warranty".
4. **#10 is the second-strongest item:** Powerfist. Princess Auto's product page lists **no** warranty length, and the store's ROYAL SERVICE Promise names **no** time limit. It's a two-sentence puzzle with a fair answer, and it's a store this audience watches. Broken Canada's Princess Auto videos drew 529K and 55K.
5. **Get to "Number ten." by about 1:00 (≈950 characters).** BTR, the 164K template, got there at 0:58. The Australian clone took 1:58 because its like/hype/share ask sat at 1:26.
6. **Hook formula.** Sentence 1: "you" plus a number. Sentence 2 opens the loop: only one covers your cordless drill for life, at number one, and it needs one thing from you. Sentence 3 is the hardest documented fact: Makita Canada's verbatim battery clause, paid at #8. Drafts are in §8.3.
7. **Honesty bridge after #10, 70 words or fewer.** The draft in §8.9 has 64 words. It says "we didn't test a drill" there, not in the hook. That was the Giant Tiger flop lesson in ct_retention.
8. **One ask, at 61–63%.** Put it after #4 (MAXIMUM) and before #3 (Milwaukee), which is the high-interest item that carries viewers across the ask. Re-tease #1 inside the ask. BTR and AFE each asked twice; the ask after the reset line is the pattern to keep.
9. **Put the ownership twist at the mid-video sag: #6 Ryobi (≈37%).** "RYOBI" is a trademark of a Japanese die-casting company. That company says it has "no capital relationship" with the maker of the tools. The twist also plants a loop that #1 pays: RIDGID's name is licensed too.
10. **Keep the comparison like for like.** BTR's most-liked comment (44 likes), plus another at 9, attacked putting a hand saw at #1 in a power-tool list. Every item must be about a **cordless drill**. Say aloud that the wrench lifetime warranties exist and don't count.
11. **Answer the viewers' recurring questions inside the items, from the warranty dossier:** the battery term, the receipt, registration, and where each brand is sold. Pre-empt "why isn't X here" in one line. The full list is in §4.
12. **Don't write in the template's register.** All three competitors read as the same script: cost-to-build claims, "charging you for a badge", motive language. The comments call that out: "AI generated emotion driven script", "Not the best researched", "This isn't research". Our edge is quoting the paper.

## 2. METHOD (and its limits)

- **Transcripts.** These are YouTube caption tracks fetched with Algrow `fetch_transcript`. Auto-caption punctuation is machine-inserted, so sentence statistics are approximate. "[music]", ">>" and "[snorts]" were stripped before measuring.
- **"First 60 seconds" = the first 160 words** (BTR's measured pace is 167 wpm; AFE's is 179 wpm).
- **Positions** are character offsets in the whitespace-normalised transcript. Times are the offset share × the API duration.
- **Oz positions are estimates** from word counts of each section, ±3 percentage points (U3).
- **Comment reading.** Comments were ranked by likes where the API returned like counts. The NexLev continuation token returned the same 20 top comments, so "newest" was pulled for the next layer of BTR comments.

## 3. COMPETITOR MAPS

### 3.1 British Truth Report (Y1): the template, 164.8K in 14 days

**First 60 seconds, verbatim (first 160 words):**
> A 180-pound cordless drill costs about 25 pounds to build. The other 155 pounds is not the motor. It is not the battery cell inside. It is a logo, a sponsorship deal, and a marketing budget sitting behind it. Half of these brands trace back to a genuine engineering story, a garage inventor, a wartime factory, a family workshop, and they never stop reminding you of it long after the story stopped having much to do with what's inside the box. Today, we are ranking 12 tool brands sold across the UK. Seven of them are quietly charging you for a badge and an advertising campaign. Five are actually putting your money into the tool itself. Stay until the end because the brand that comes out on top is not the one you're expecting. It isn't even a name most people in this country have heard of. And it might change which aisle you walk down next time. Let's start

**What the hook does:**
- S1: a number claim with no source (cost to build).
- S2–4: a reveal of where the money goes.
- S5: a heritage line.
- Then the count ("12… Seven… Five"), stated as the title has it.
- The loop is vague: "not the one you're expecting… a name most people… have heard of".
- **No "you" in sentence 1.** "You" appears 1.09 times per 100 words across the video, the lowest in our samples.

Whole video: 19,329 characters, 3,307 words, 167 wpm. Mean sentence 17.3 words; 21% under 8 words; 22% over 25. No comment prompt in the narration. A pinned owner comment carries product links and the comment question.

**Per-item structure (repeated in every item):**
1. Place plus name ("11th place, Stanley.").
2. Heritage or familiarity anchor.
3. Pivot line ("Here's the uncomfortable part" / "But here's the trick").
4. Parent company and range split.
5. A price range with no store or date.
6. A contrast verdict ("The 1843 story isn't lying to you. It's just doing the heavy lifting…").
7. A history coda paragraph.

**Re-hooks:** almost none between items. Most transitions are a bare "Nth place, Brand." The working devices are:
- the "Let's start with the brand whose…" opener;
- the reset line before the flip ("Six brands down, all built on the same trick");
- the promise attached to the second ask ("the next five brands might genuinely change what's in your toolbox").

**Payoff:** #1 at 88% delivers the "name you haven't heard of" promise. But it is a hand-saw maker in a power-tool list. The most-liked viewer comment is about exactly that mismatch (§4).

### 3.2 Oz Grocery Alert (Y2): a clone, 19.96K in 11 days

**First 60 seconds, verbatim (first 160 words):**
> Have you ever wondered why an old corded grinder from decades ago still screams to life the second you flick the switch? While your brand new cordless drill dies a quiet death right in the middle of a weekend decking job just because a cheap plastic battery latch snapped. You are about to find out exactly where your cash goes when you walk down the central aisle at Bunnings. I'm going to show you how a staggering chunk of the price tag on a shiny power tool has nothing to do with copper wire or steel gearboxes and everything to do with corporate marketing budgets. We will crack open 12 tool brands sold across Australia separating engineering triumphs from pure corporate slide of hand. By the time we finish, you will know more about the industry than 90% of contractors loading up their utes. Stay until the end because the brand that takes the crown isn't some flashy import with

**What the hook does:**
- A "you" scene (a cordless drill failing), which is a good S1 type.
- A durability implication, which is barred for us.
- A count, then an unsourced "90% of contractors".
- The same vague "under the radar name" loop as BTR.
- **Then about 90 seconds of "economics"** (the $250 / $25 build-cost claim and motive lines) before item 1. Estimated first item: ≈13–14%, **≈2:50**.

**Item order** (estimated positions):
1. Bosch green (labelled "seventh place from the bottom").
2. Stanley (labelled "11th place overall", a **miscount**: the first item should have been 12th).
3. Trojan (Bunnings house brand).
4. Black & Decker.
5. Ryobi.
6. DeWalt.
7. Milwaukee.
8. **One ask (≈63%)**: "If you have made it this far, hit subscribe now".
9. Then #5 Ferrex/Workzone (Aldi), #4 Kincrome, #3 Bosch Professional, #2 Makita, #1 Thomas Flinn (the same #1 as BTR, a Sheffield saw maker, "available locally through… Carbatec").

**Per-item structure:** the same as BTR, but longer pivots and heavier motive language ("corporate slide of hand", "an accountant decided…").

**Tail:**
- six numbered buying rules;
- a thesis paragraph;
- a comment prompt ("Which of these tool brands is sitting in your shed right now…?");
- a next-video tease;
- a sign-off line.

**Lesson (OUR NOTE).** Same title formula, same order, same #1, and about one-eighth of the template's views in a comparable window. A clone of the structure does not travel on its own. Topic and market fit matter as much (U6).

### 3.3 Australia Food Exposed (Y3): a longer clone, 164 views at about 2 hours old

**Current views and age:** 164 views, 18 likes and 2 comments, at about 2 h 17 min after publication (2026-10-05 23:00:21 UTC). Too young to judge. Re-pull it at 24 h and 72 h if we want a benchmark (U5).

**First 60 seconds, verbatim (first 160 words):**
> $15 million. That's what the federal court ordered the company behind one of the most hyped tool brands in Australia to pay. Not for a dodgy battery, not for a faulty drill, for telling retailers they weren't allowed to sell its tools any cheaper. While you were standing in the aisle wondering why a drill costs what it costs, someone had already decided the answer for you. And that's just one brand. Here's the thing most Australians never stop to think about. When you pay $400 for a cordless kit, a big slice of that money isn't going into the motor. the gearbox or the cells inside the battery. It's going into a logo, a sponsorship deal, a shelf position, a carefully managed price floor. Some of the most famous names on the wall at Bunnings were built by real inventors in real workshops. Then they were bought, merged, rebadged, and squeezed until the name was worth more than the

**What the hook does:**
- S1 is a one-number fragment. S2 names a court as the source.
- The strongest device in the set: **a hook figure paid at a named later position.** At 50.9% (#6 Milwaukee) the narrator says "remember that number from the very start of this video. This is where it comes from."
- A count at 3.1% (0:57).
- Then a like/hype/share ask at **4.7% (1:26)**, which pushes item 1 to **1:58**.
- The ask contains "harder to keep the truth hidden", a banned word for us.

**What AFE added to the template that suits us:**
- **Warranty terms per brand.** AEG, Ryobi, Makita, Kincrome and Ferrex each get their term, said with the registration window.
- **Fairness clauses inside the attack** ("Now, to be fair…", "Now, credit where it's due.").
- **A #1 that is the same category as the national-pride angle** (a local maker).
- **A rule to "register your tools" within 30 days.** OUR NOTE: this is the nearest competitor beat to our #1 condition.

**What to avoid:** the 30:53 runtime, two asks plus an early hype ask, and motive lines ("It was policy.").

### 3.4 Cross-video lessons (OUR NOTE)

| Lesson | Evidence | Our move |
|---|---|---|
| First item by about 1:00 is achievable, even with a count and a loop | BTR at 0:58 | Our first item at ≈950 characters (§8.2) |
| An early hype ask delays item 1 by about 1 min | AFE's ask at 1:26 pushed item 1 to 1:58 | One ask only, at 61–63% |
| A hook figure paid at a named position works as a mid-video re-hook | AFE "$15 million" → paid at 50.9% | Hook S3 (the Makita clause) is paid at #8, ≈4:50 |
| A vague "#1 you won't expect" loop is weaker than a specific one | BTR, Oz | Our loop names the prize: "covers your cordless drill for life… needs one thing from you" |
| Keep #1 in the same category | BTR's top comment: 44 likes; another at 9 | Every slot is a cordless drill |
| A reset line before the flip carries the ask | BTR 60.9%, AFE 56.9% | Our ask is at 61.6% with a #1 re-tease, then straight into #3 |
| Items run 1,100–1,900 characters | BTR mean ≈1,390; AFE mean ≈1,615 | Ours ≈1,700; #1 up to 2,200 |

## 4. WHAT VIEWERS ARGUED ABOUT OR ASKED (retention gold)

Comments are tier (c). They are used only to choose which questions the script answers. Like counts are as returned on 2026-10-06.

| # | Theme | Evidence (verbatim excerpt, likes) | What the script should do |
|---|---|---|---|
| V1 | **Apples to oranges at #1** | "Comparing the No 1 - hand saw brand with the other 9 power tool brand - is like comparing a cheese board with the grapes." (44) · "Inserting a hand saw as a quality tool after reviewing a series of battery powered tools is NONSENSE…" (9) | Every slot compares the **same object**, a cordless drill. Say once: "Several of these brands promise lifetime on wrenches. We're only counting the drill." (tl_warranty §12: hand-tool lifetimes at Craftsman, DeWalt, Milwaukee, Ryobi, Mastercraft, MAXIMUM) |
| V2 | **Budget brands defended on value** | "Parkside offers by far the best price per use for occasional home use." (40) · "their 3 year warranty and great customer service, speaks volumes" | The warranty term per dollar is a fair question. Pull unit prices from `tl_prices.md`; don't judge value as quality |
| V3 | **"You missed brand X" / "not research"** | "How can you put this out without even mentioning Festool? … This isn't research…" (24, 6 replies) · "what about Sealey tools" · "Not the best researched article." | One line naming **why these ten** (sold at Canadian Tire, Home Depot, RONA, Princess Auto) and **which alternates we also checked** (Black and Decker, Kobalt, Hyper Tough). It is in the bridge (§8.9) |
| V4 | **Battery platforms are contested** | "Have had 2 Makita cordless drills for over 20 years… but the battery design has changed 3 times since." (22, 5 replies) · "So Ryobi is bad for having a battery fitment that hasn't changed… But Makita is good…" | Don't make compatibility claims unless they come from the brand's own page (see `tl_platform.md`). Our battery beat is the **warranty term**, plus Makita's verbatim battery clause |
| V5 | **Durability anecdotes contradict "avoid" verdicts** | Several: "my 15 year old Bosch… still operates perfectly", "Bosch Green… None of these have ever broken" | We rank paper, not durability. Say so in the bridge. It defuses this whole comment class |
| V6 | **Warranty turnaround and swap convenience** | "they got out of the 'big' names because if it needed warranty they went without their tool for weeks. By getting Ryobi they just drop into bunnings and swap for new one" (6) | High-interest, but **not researched**. Don't claim turnaround times. Where the terms say where to go (Mastercraft: "redeemable at any Canadian Tire store"; Ryobi 90-day exchange "to the dealer from which it was purchased"), say it. U12 |
| V7 | **Factual pushback on specs** | "Both Bosch Green and Ryobi tools are now mostly brushless…" (6) | No brushed/brushless verdicts. Quality and performance claims are barred anyway |
| V8 | **Is it even sold here?** | "Didnt realise milwaukee was sold at bunnings" (2 replies) | Put the Canadian retailer on screen for each brand (from tl_warranty "Sold in Canada" lines) |
| V9 | **Does a warranty survive an ownership change?** | "The law says that if the owners of the company change there's no requirement to honour the warranty… Snap on has its own cheaper brand called Blue point still having the lifetime guarantee" (Y3) | **Don't answer the legal question** (not researched; U13). The Craftsman item can state the 2017 sale and today's V20 term, and nothing about older tools |
