## 6. COMPETITOR CLAIMS THAT WOULD BREAK OUR RULES (flag list) AND PHRASES NOT TO COPY

### 6.1 Rule breaks

| # | Video | Claim (verbatim or close) | Rule broken | Our handling |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F1 | Y1 | "A 180-pound cordless drill costs about 25 pounds to build." | Unsourced figure; non-Canadian currency; implies motive | Never use any cost-to-build figure |
| F2 | Y2 | "A standard consumer cordless drill retailing for $250 costs roughly 25 to build…" | Unsourced; non-Canadian | Same |
| F3 | Y1, Y3 | "Seven of them are quietly charging you for a badge…" / "10 of them are quietly charging you…" | Banned word "quietly"; implies motive | Never characterise any brand's pricing intent |
| F4 | Y1, Y3 | "But here's the trick." / "a trick built into the way you compare prices" | Banned word "trick" | Use "But here's what the page says." |
| F5 | Y3 | "…the harder it becomes to keep the truth hidden." | Banned word "hidden"; implies concealment | n/a |
| F6 | Y1, Y2, Y3 | "plastic housing that sits in the same tier as unbranded imports", "not meaningfully better built", "without matching their build quality", "genuinely excellent", "battery cells rated for significantly fewer total charge cycles" | Quality, durability and performance stated as fact; no named tester; no model match | We rank the written warranty only |
| F7 | Y2 | "…an accountant decided replacing a steel gear with a powdered alloy saves 40 cents per unit…" | Invented motive and figure | n/a |
| F8 | Y1 | "Erbauer… a name a marketing team picked because it… sounds vaguely Bavarian" | Motive attribution | n/a |
| F9 | Y1, Y2 | Parkside "built by… Germany's Grizzly tools"; Trojan "sourced… predominantly throughout industrial centers in China" | Naming or inferring a house-brand supplier | **Never** name or infer a maker for Mastercraft, MAXIMUM or Powerfist (tl_ownership D1) |
| F10 | Y1 | "Testing of Parkside products has traced more than 10 different supplier companies…" | Unnamed tester | n/a |
| F11 | Y1, Y2, Y3 | "DIY forums are full of…", "Bargain hunters tracked…", "ask tradies… you'll hear the same story" | Unattributed sources | n/a |
| F12 | Y1 | "…swallowed by a conglomerate and gutted the way Craftsman and Ted Baker were" | Implies wrongdoing | Craftsman: state the 2017 SBD purchase [S3] and say it is a disclosed commercial deal (tl_ownership D8) |
| F13 | Y1 / Y3 | TTI bought Milwaukee "in 2005 for around 626 million US" (Y1) vs "a 2004 deal worth around 626 million US" (Y3) | U.S. figure; the two sources conflict | Don't use |
| F14 | Y3 | "So for years when you saw Milwaukee at roughly the same price wherever you looked, that wasn't a coincidence. It was policy." / "the price fixing penalty" | Inference beyond the record; mislabels a resale-price-maintenance penalty as "price fixing"; Australian matter | **Never import ACCC or Australian Federal Court matters into a Canadian script** |
| F15 | Y3 | Snap-on weekly accounts aimed at "a first-year apprentice on apprentice wages…" | Implies predatory motive | n/a |
| F16 | Y2 | "you will know more about the industry than 90% of contractors" | Unsourced | n/a |
| F17 | Y1, Y2 | "the brand that comes out on top… isn't even a name most people… have heard of" | Unfalsifiable loop | Name a checkable prize instead |
| F18 | Y4 | FTC "lifetime" rule and Magnuson-Moss (U.S. law) | U.S. law presented to a general audience | Don't present U.S. warranty law as Canadian. Provincial law was not researched (U14) |
| F19 | Y2 | Labelling "seventh place from the bottom" then "11th place overall" | Miscount (Rule 3 / script-rules §3 item 7) | Our count must match the title: "10", "Number ten." to "Number one." |

### 6.2 Template phrases not to reuse

These are recognisable across Y1–Y3; viewers will spot a clone.
- "ROBBING You Blind (And 5 That Are Actually Worth It)"
- "Stay until the end because the brand…"
- "Say the name out loud."
- "isn't lying to you. It's just doing the heavy lifting"
- "Quick pause before we carry on / keep going"
- "Quick reset before we flip the list"
- "Now we're switching sides completely"
- "If you've made it this far, tap subscribe now"
- "Your toolbox is either an investment or an invoice"
- "charging you for a badge"
- "Here's the uncomfortable part / truth"
- "ecosystem tax"
- "Stay critical of the badge"

## 7. RECOMMENDED RANKING (warranty yardstick, ordered for retention)

### 7.1 The rule (say it in the promise beat)

1. **Primary:** the length of written coverage on the cordless drill, as stated on the brand's own Canadian page, or on the Canadian retailer's listing for house brands.
2. **Tie-break 1:** the battery term.
3. **Tie-break 2:** written extras (free service, money-back or exchange windows).
4. **Tie-break 3:** conditions that cost you something (registration deadline, receipt, battery-brand clauses).

OUR NOTE: this rule produces the order below without forcing anything. The only judgement calls are the ties inside the 3-year group and within the 5-year group, where retention breaks the tie (stated in the table).

### 7.2 Order

All warranty facts are from tl_warranty [R#]; ownership facts from tl_ownership [S#]. Character positions follow the beat sheet in §8.2.

|---|---|---|---|---|

We guarantee to make it right. We will repair, replace or refund any product to your satisfaction." No time limit stated [R29] | No stated term, so it is last on the yardstick. **Say it isn't "no warranty" and isn't "lifetime"** (tl_warranty §0.3) | **Second-strongest.** A puzzle that is answered fairly inside 90 s. High-interest store (Broken Canada Princess Auto: 529K and 55K). The ownership line is warm (a Canadian family since 1942 [S32]) |

PWR POD batteries and charger **sold separately**: "2 year exchange warranty". Receipt: "Returns, exchanges or warranties on an item without a receipt may not be accepted." ### 7.3 Fallbacks

- **Craftsman fails the "sold in Canada now" check** (no retailer product page captured): drop it and promote **Black and Decker**. Canadian Tire lists BDCDD120C-2 (0547286P) as "1 year exchange warranty" [R31]. On the yardstick it then sits at **#9**, and Mastercraft moves to #8, Makita to #7. The title count stays at 10.
- **Powerfist #10 feels too harsh in the edit:** swap Mastercraft to #10 and Powerfist to #9. This is weaker for retention, because #10 loses the puzzle. Keep the yardstick sentence either way, and say Powerfist's slot reflects "no number on the page", not a judgement on the store.
- **MAXIMUM listings change again before upload:** keep MAXIMUM at #4 only if a current brushless kit listing still shows "5 year limited". Otherwise it falls to the 3-year group (repair-only 1-year kits would put it near #9) and the ask needs a new high-interest neighbour.

## 8. BEAT SHEET (adapted from ct_retention §5)

### 8.1 House constraints (carried over)

- 21,000–23,000 characters of narration. Target ≈21,800.
- Exactly one ask containing **"take a second to subscribe"**, at 61–63% of characters.
- **"the truth is not always on the menu"** exactly twice: at the end of the hook and as the last spoken line. OUR NOTE: the menu image is odd for tools. If the producer drops it, update `tl_ret_check.py` line 18.
- One no-commercial-relationship disclosure.
- A "How to protect yourself" section with First/Second/Third/Fourth.
- No sentence over 40 words.
- None of: secret, hidden, quietly, caught, exposed, scam, trick.
- No "we tested/bought/visited".
- Every legal or official item carries its label.
- No comment ask mid-video.
- Recalls and safety notices never appear (the DO-NOT-USE list only).

### 8.2 Beat table (≈975 characters per minute = 165 wpm × 5.9 characters per word)

| # | Beat | Characters (start–end) | Budget | % at start | Time | Job |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| B0 | Cold open (§8.3) | 0–700 | 700 | 0% | 0:00 | "You" plus a number; L1; the Makita clause (L2); fairness; tagline #1 |
| B1 | Promise and yardstick (§8.4) | 700–950 | 250 | 3.2% | 0:43 | Count "ten… from ten down to one"; the rule; batteries separate; "number ten has no number" |
| B2 | **#10 Powerfist** | 950–2,650 | 1,700 | 4.4% | **0:58** | Second-strongest |
| B3 | Honesty bridge, ≤70 words (§8.9) | 2,650–3,020 | 370 | 12.2% | 2:43 | Paper, not performance; why these ten; alternates checked |
| B4 | #9 Mastercraft | 3,020–4,720 | 1,700 | 13.9% | 3:06 | Mantra #1: "two numbers" |
| B5 | #8 Makita | 4,720–6,420 | 1,700 | 21.7% | 4:50 | **Pays L2** (hook S3) |
| B6 | #7 Craftsman | 6,420–8,120 | 1,700 | 29.4% | 6:35 | Lifetime on wrenches vs three on drills |
| B7 | #6 Ryobi | 8,120–9,870 | 1,750 | 37.2% | 8:20 | **Ownership twist at the sag**; plants L3 |
| B8 | #5 DeWalt (opens with the halfway re-hook, ≈125 characters) | 9,870–11,570 | 1,700 | 45.3% | 10:07 | Re-tease L1 |
| B9 | #4 MAXIMUM | 11,570–13,270 | 1,700 | 53.1% | 11:52 | Climb into the ask |
| B10 | **Ask** (§8.10) | 13,270–13,550 | 280 | 60.9% | 13:37 | Phrase at ≈13,420 = **61.6%** |
| B11 | **#3 Milwaukee** | 13,550–15,350 | 1,800 | 62.2% | 13:54 | High interest, carries viewers past the ask |
| B12 | #2 Bosch | 15,350–17,150 | 1,800 | 70.4% | 15:45 | Shelf vs maker; sets protect step 4 |
| B13 | **#1 RIDGID** | 17,150–19,350 | 2,200 | 78.7% | 17:35 | Pays L1 and L3; the condition stated three ways |
| B14 | How to protect yourself (four steps, §8.11) | 19,350–20,550 | 1,200 | 88.8% | 19:51 | Mantra #2 at step 1 |
| B15 | Close: recap run, comment prompt, next tease, mantra #3, tagline #2 | 20,550–21,250 | 700 | 94.3% | 21:05 | Keep it short |
| B16 | Sources, disclosure, "prices and terms change" line | 21,250–21,800 | 550 | 97.5% | 21:48 | |

**Arithmetic check (OUR NOTE):** 13,420 / 21,800 = 61.56%. If the total runs long, the ask must move with it. At 23,000 total, the phrase must sit between 14,030 and 14,490.

**Pace:** at 165 wpm the runtime is ≈22:22. Time the TTS voice on a 1,000-character sample first (U4).

**Rhythm targets** (from ct_retention): mean sentence 12–16 words; ≥20% under 8 words; ≤12% over 25 words; ≤3 spoken figures per 100 words (put product numbers and model codes in lower-thirds); "you" ≥2.5 per 100 words.

### 8.3 Hook drafts (S1 "you" plus a number · S2 the loop · S3 the hardest documented fact)

**Draft A (recommended; 124 words, 683 characters, longest sentence 30 words):**
> You can choose from at least ten drill brands at Canadian Tire, Home Depot, RONA and Princess Auto, and on October sixth we read the Canadian warranty for each one. Only one of the ten covers your cordless drill for life. It's number one, and it needs one thing from you first. Another brand's own Canadian warranty page says this, word for word. "The use of non-Makita brand batteries will void all warranties for these products." That one comes back at number eight. Every company here sets its own terms, and none of this says anyone did anything wrong. So we read each brand's own page, and the store's listing where a brand has none. Because the truth is not always on the menu.

Fact basis:
- "at least ten drill brands… Canadian Tire, Home Depot, RONA and Princess Auto": the "Sold in Canada" lines in tl_warranty. Craftsman/RONA depends on U10. If Craftsman drops, say "Canadian Tire, Home Depot and Princess Auto".
- "Only one of the ten covers your cordless drill for life… needs one thing from you": tl_warranty §0 and §1 (LSA; register within 90 days with receipt) [R1, R4, R5].
- The Makita sentence: verbatim, makita.ca Warranty Statement [R11].
- Note: S3 names Makita before #8. That is deliberate. AFE showed a hook fact paid at a named position works.

**Draft B (100 words; no brand named in the hook; use it if the producer wants Makita's name held back):**
> If you own a cordless drill, the warranty that came with it is probably on a page you've never opened, so on October sixth we read the Canadian pages for ten brands. Only one of the ten covers your cordless drill for life, and we'll show you the one step it needs at number one. One brand's page says that using another company's batteries "will void all warranties". Another lists no warranty length at all. Every company here sets its own terms, and none of this says anyone did anything wrong. Because the truth is not always on the menu.

Fact basis: as Draft A, plus Powerfist [R29]. "probably on a page you've never opened" is rhetoric about the viewer, not a claim about a company. Cut it if the editor objects.

**Don't:**
- open with a cost-to-build figure, heritage, "today we expose", or a methods disclaimer;
- name RIDGID before #1;
- say "lifetime warranty".

### 8.4 Promise and yardstick (39 words, measured)

> Here's the plan. Ten brands, ranked by how long each one covers a cordless drill in writing, from ten down to one. Batteries are counted separately, because they often get less. And number ten has no number at all.

"Batteries… often get less": Mastercraft (2 vs 3), Milwaukee (2–3 vs 5) and Bosch (3 vs 5) in tl_warranty §12. Keep "often", not "always": Makita, Ryobi and Craftsman are equal.

### 8.5 Open-loop plan

Rule: every loop is paid on air. ct_retention U3 shows the cost of an unpaid loop.

| Loop | Planted | Wording (draft) | Re-teased | Paid |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **L1 (main)** | Hook S2, ≈0:08 | "Only one of the ten covers your cordless drill for life… it needs one thing from you first." | #5 halfway line (45%); the ask (61.6%) | **#1** (≈79–88%): the LSA plus the 90-day registration |
| **L2 (early)** | Hook S3, ≈0:20 | The Makita battery sentence, "comes back at number eight" | #9 re-hook ("Number eight is the sentence from the start of this video") | **#8** (≈22%, 4:50) |
| **L3 (twist)** | End of #6 Ryobi, ≈10:00 | "The green drill's name has one owner. The tools have another. Keep that in mind for number one." | Halfway line | **#1**: "RIDGID is a registered trademark of RIDGID, Inc., used under license." [S20] |
| **L4 (puzzle)** | Promise, ≈0:55 | "number ten has no number at all" | none | **#10** within 30 s |
| **L5 (protect)** | #2 Bosch | "screenshot the maker's page; you'll see why in a minute" | none | Protect step 4 |

Optional extra twist for #1 (**verify first**, U11): the RYOBI warranty PDF names One World Technologies, Anderson SC [R6]. The RIDGID drill manual names the same company [S20]. That is two primary documents naming one company. Do **not** add TTI; that link is unverified (tl_ownership U1).

Bosch's page is the one Bosch writes." | "On the day you buy, screenshot the maker's warranty page." | "Here's where the drill covered for life comes back." |
| #1 | "Number one. RIDGID. For life, with one condition." | "You get home with the orange box, and the clock starts." | "RIDGID's own registration site calls it 'a promotional program'. Those are its words." | "Register within 90 days. Upload the receipt. Re-register any replacement within 90 days." | → protect |

Labels for #1:
- Quote "Lifetime Service Agreement" and "FREE Batteries. FREE Parts. FREE Service. FOR LIFE." as RIDGID's.
- Attribute "Industry's Only Lifetime Service Agreement" to Home Depot Canada's listing [R13], not to us.
- Standalone batteries: the sources conflict, so don't claim they're covered (tl_warranty §1).

### 8.7 Per-item micro-structure (≈1,700 characters; #1 up to 2,200)

1. **Headline (≤12 words):** "Number N. Brand. [the number or the hook]". It names a thing and makes no claim.
2. **"You" scene (1–2 sentences, ≤40 words):** where the viewer meets this brand (the aisle, the box, the garage).
3. **The terms (3–5 sentences):** tool term, then battery term, then the one condition. Verbatim and attributed ("Makita Canada's warranty statement says…"). Read on Oct 6. At most 3 spoken figures. Product codes go in the lower-third: "#8 · makita.ca warranty statement · read Oct 6, 2026".
4. **Ownership or where-sold line (1–2 sentences):** from tl_ownership. A disclosed fact, and say so when it is a licence or sale ("that's a normal licence", "a disclosed deal").
5. **Fairness (1 sentence):** the company's own words, or the limit of what we checked.
6. **The fix (1 sentence):** an action the viewer can take this week.
7. **Re-hook (≤20 words):** into the next number (§8.8).

Budget: headline 60 · scene 200 · terms 750 · ownership 250 · fairness 180 · fix 140 · re-hook 120 = **≈1,700**.

**Skeleton for #10, structure only.** The writer fills it from tl_warranty §10 and tl_ownership §3.3.
> Number ten. Powerfist. The drill with no number. [scene: at Princess Auto, turning the box over, ≤25 words] [terms: on October sixth, Princess Auto's pages for three Powerfist twenty-volt drills listed no warranty length; what they show is the ROYAL SERVICE Promise, verbatim; it names no time limit] [ownership: Princess Auto, Winnipeg, the same family since 1942 [S32]] [fairness: "We're not calling it lifetime, and we're not calling it no warranty."] The fix: before you pay, ask the counter how that promise applies to a drill and its battery. [re-hook]

### 8.8 Re-hook lines (channel voice)

Drafts measured in `tl/ret/drafts.txt`: longest 20 words, no banned words. Lines 5, 6 and 8 were reworded after the measurement and are still under 20 words.
1. "Number ten had no number. Number nine has two, and they don't match."
2. "Keep the receipt from that one. You'll need a receipt again at number one."
3. "Number eight is the sentence from the start of this video."
4. "That's what Makita wrote. Here's what you can do about it."
5. "So far, the name on each box has belonged to the company whose warranty we just read. Number six is different."
6. "The green drill's name has one owner. The tools have another. Keep that in mind for number one."
7. "Five to go, and the next one adds two things the others don't."
8. "Number four is the first brand on this list to put five years in writing, and it's sold only at Canadian Tire."
9. "Three left. The next one gives the tool five years and the battery less."
10. "Number two is the one where the shelf and the maker don't say the same thing."
11. "Here's where the drill covered for life comes back."
12. "Easy to miss. Easy to fix. Here's the fix."

Fact checks on the lines:
- Line 5: Craftsman (SBD), Makita, Mastercraft (CTC, listed in the AIF) and Powerfist (Princess Auto) each own their names [S1, S33, S3, S40].
- Line 6: RYOBI Limited licence [S12, S16].
- Line 8: MAXIMUM is the first 5-year term in countdown order, because #10–#5 are ≤3 years. "Sold only at Canadian Tire" follows the safe wording "Exclusive to Canadian Tire" [S30].
- Line 9: Milwaukee [R10].

### 8.9 Honesty bridge (after #10; 64 words, measured)

> Quick honesty check before number nine. We didn't test a single drill, so this ranks what's written down, not how a tool holds up. Every term comes from the brand's own Canadian page, or the store's listing where there isn't one, read on October sixth. We also checked Black and Decker, Kobalt and Hyper Tough. Terms change, so check the box before you pay.

- This line pre-empts comment classes V3 and V5 (§4).
- "We also checked… Kobalt and Hyper Tough": tl_warranty §11 says their Canadian terms are **UNVERIFIED** (RONA and Walmart blocked the fetch). "Checked" is true as an attempt. If the editor wants precision, say "We also looked at…".

**Disclosure (once, in B16):**
> Canadian Counter has no commercial relationship with any brand or store in this video, and none of them reviewed it before release.

### 8.10 The mid-list ask (B10; 53 words, measured; phrase at ≈61.6%)

> Three left, and the last of them is the only one on this list that covers your drill for life. If you want the next Canadian aisle checked against the paperwork like this, take a second to subscribe. Canadian Counter reads the fine print so you don't have to. Now, the red tools.

The pattern is a loop re-tease, then the ask, then an immediate payoff. That is the Costco hit's pattern (ct_retention V8). No comment ask here.

### 8.11 The tail

**How to protect yourself (four steps, ≈1,200 characters):**
- **First:** read two numbers, the drill's and the battery's. This is mantra #2. Callback to Mastercraft and Milwaukee.
- **Second:** write the purchase date on the box and keep the receipt. Canadian Tire's "may not be accepted"; RIDGID's receipt upload; DeWalt's 90 days.
- **Third:** register when the warranty needs it. RIDGID: 90 days, and re-register replacements. Milwaukee says registration is "not necessary".
- **Fourth:** read the maker's own page, not only the shelf tag (Bosch), and its battery rule (Makita). This pays L5.

**Close (≈700 characters):**
1. One-sentence recap run, for example: "Ten brands: one with no number, five at three years, three at five, and one for life, if you register."
   - OUR NOTE on the count: Mastercraft, Makita, Craftsman, Ryobi and DeWalt = five at 3 years; MAXIMUM (this listing), Milwaukee and Bosch = three at 5 years; RIDGID = 1; Powerfist = 1. That totals 10.
   - If Black and Decker replaces Craftsman, change it to "one at one year, four at three".
2. **Comment prompt (Rule 16: data the viewer already has):** "Tell us the brand of the drill you use most, how many years you've had it, and whether you've ever made a warranty claim on it."
3. Next-video tease (one sentence; topic to be set by the producer).
4. Mantra #3: "Read two numbers: the drill's and the battery's."
5. **Tagline #2 as the last spoken line before the sources paragraph.**

**Chapters (OUR judgement, untested):** hold description chapters for the first 48 hours. The thumbnail says "ONLY 1 FOR LIFE", and chapters would let viewers jump straight to #1.

### 8.12 Pronunciation and visuals (from comments V10)

**Pronunciation:**
- BLACK+DECKER → "Black and Decker"
- RIDGID → "ridged"
- RYOBI → "rye-OH-bee" (AFE's captions rendered it "Robi")
- MAXIMUM → "maximum", even though it is styled in capitals
- Makita → "mah-KEE-tah"
- Powerfist → "power fist"

**Visuals:** Canadian store exteriors and shelves only; bilingual packaging where shown.

**Lower-thirds:** carry the product codes and URLs, so the narration stays at ≤3 spoken figures per 100 words.

### 8.13 Pre-flight checker

Run:
`python3 /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-Food/63b91656-26a4-59d5-baf9-bd65edc4c0dd/scratchpad/tl/tl_ret_check.py <script.md>`

- Same checks as `ct/ret_check.py`, except the item sequence is "Number ten." through "Number one.".
- Known quirk, inherited: the per-item length printout trims only #3's chunk before the ask. In this layout the ask comes after #4, so **#4's reported length will include the ask's ≈280 characters**. Read it with that in mind.

## GAP FIXES (critic)

Added 2026-10-06. Full reasoning is in tl_gaps.md.

1. **Title.** "Covers Your Cordless Drill for Life" states coverage as unconditional. RIDGID's is conditional: registration within 90 days, receipt upload, "a promotional program", and reconditioned tools excluded. Recommended title: **"We Investigated 10 Tool Brands Sold in Canada (Only 1 Offers Lifetime Coverage on Cordless Drills)"** (97 characters). The thumbnail "ONLY 1 FOR LIFE" can stay.
2. **#4 MAXIMUM.** Its brand warranty page adds a 90-day satisfaction guarantee and a 1-year repair and service warranty for cordless tools, with "UP TO 5 YEARS LIMITED WARRANTY" (tl_warranty GF-W1). Say "up to five years".
   - MAXIMUM can now be compared on 5-year tool terms with Milwaukee and Bosch. Under tie-break 2 it has more written extras than either.
   - Moving MAXIMUM above #3 Milwaukee is defensible, but the ask's high-interest neighbour should stay Milwaukee.
   - Keep the order unless the writer wants to re-run the rule.
3. **Re-hook line 8.** "the first brand on this list to put five years in writing" stays true in countdown order. Add "up to".
4. **Hook Draft A** keeps "RONA". Craftsman stays in (tl_gaps.md §4).
5. **Recap count** is unchanged: five at three years, three at up to five, one for life with registration, one with no number.
6. **Powerfist #10.** Now add "and the drill's own manual" (tl_warranty GF-W3).
7. **#1 RIDGID.** Add the reconditioned-tool exclusion to the condition list (tl_warranty GF-W5).

# COMPLETENESS CRITIC REPORT: 10 tool brands sold in Canada

Video: "We Investigated 10 Tool Brands Sold in Canada (Only 1 Covers Your Cordless Drill for Life)". Upload 2026-10-07.
Compiled 2026-10-06. My own captures ran from 01:30 to 01:45 UTC on 2026-10-06, which is the evening of 2026-10-05 in Toronto. Local copies are in `scratchpad/gap/`.
Dossiers read in full: tl_ownership.md, tl_warranty.md, tl_prices.md, tl_platform.md, tl_record.md, tl_retention.md, and the house rules in /home/user/Food/script_rules_and_prompt.md.
Each fix below was appended to the relevant dossier under the heading "GAP FIXES (critic)".

---

## 0. VERDICT IN FIVE LINES

1. **"Only 1" is proven in its written-terms form.** Each of the other nine brands' Canadian pages either states a finite term for cordless drills or, for Powerfist, states no term. RIDGID's Canadian page is the only one that puts "FOR LIFE" on a cordless drill.
2. **"Covers… for Life" is not proven as an unconditional statement.** RIDGID's coverage depends on registration within 90 days with a receipt upload. RIDGID's own registration site calls it "a promotional program" and excludes reconditioned tools.
3. **All ten core brands are confirmed on sale in Canada.** For Craftsman, the evidence is RONA's own current flyer (nine Craftsman power-tool items in Toronto, Calgary and Vancouver) plus craftsman.ca. A rona.ca page capture is still blocked (403).
4. **Final ten: keep Craftsman.** Black and Decker stays the ready-made fallback.
5. **Title: amend it.** Use "We Investigated 10 Tool Brands Sold in Canada (Only 1 Offers Lifetime Coverage on Cordless Drills)" (97 characters). Thumbnail "ONLY 1 FOR LIFE" can stay.

## 2. THE KEY CLAIM: "only RIDGID gives lifetime coverage on cordless tools among the 10"

Test: does each brand's own Canadian page (or, for house brands, the Canadian retailer's page) put a lifetime term on a cordless drill?

| Brand | Longest written term for a cordless drill (Canadian source) | Any "lifetime" on that brand's pages? | Covers a cordless drill? | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| RIDGID | "FREE Batteries. FREE Parts. FREE Service. FOR LIFE." Register within 90 days and upload the receipt. Without registration: "automatic 3-year limited manufacturer warranty". | LSA; also hand tools | **Yes, conditional** | Proven (R1, R4, R5, R13). New caveat: "Reconditioned tools are not eligible for the Lifetime Service Agreement" (GF-W5). "Life" is not defined (U9). |
| Ryobi | 3-year limited (PDF linked from ryobitools.ca) | Non-powered hand tools | No | Proven (R6, R7) |
| DeWalt | 3-year limited + 1-year free service + 90-day money back | Mechanics tools, hand tools (capped at one year for anything electrical), masonry bits; 7-year exists for unnamed products | No | Proven (R8; GF-W7) |
| Milwaukee | 5 years on the tool; batteries 2 or 3 years | Hand tools, non-rechargeable lighting, LED in the work light, Hex-LOK handle, HOLE DOZER, INSIDER socket, REDSTICK level; "does not cover battery packs or all power tools" | No | Proven (R10; GF-W7) |
| Makita | 3 years on lithium-ion tools, batteries and chargers | None | No | Proven (R11) |
| Bosch | 18V tools 5 years, batteries 3 years (purchases from 6/1/23) | Hand tools (H-series) | No | Proven (R20; GF-W6) |
| Craftsman | V20: "3 Year Limited Warranty" | Hand and mechanics tools: "Full lifetime warranty" | No | Proven (R23–R25; GF-W8) |
| Mastercraft | "3 year exchange warranty redeemable at any Canadian Tire store" on all 10 cordless drill kits at store 150; manual: 3 years, batteries and chargers 2 | Hand tools: "lifetime exchange" | No | **Proven by today's full sweep** (GF-W2) |
| MAXIMUM | Brand page: cordless "UP TO 5 YEARS LIMITED WARRANTY"; listings vary (1-yr repair-only, 5-yr, "special warranty") | Manual cutting, fastening and metal-working tools, sockets and wrenches: "LIFETIME WARRANTY… no time limit" | No | **Proven at brand level** (GF-W1). Two kits' listings (0548799P, 4994235P) still say only "special warranty". |
| Powerfist | Product pages and **both manuals**: no warranty length. ROYAL SERVICE Promise: "We will repair, replace or refund any product to your satisfaction", no time limit stated, word "lifetime" absent | None on Princess Auto's pages | Not stated | Proven as "no stated term" (R29; GF-W3, GF-W4) |

**Conclusion.**
- Written-terms form: **proven**. Among the ten, only RIDGID's own Canadian page puts lifetime ("FOR LIFE") coverage on a cordless drill.
- Not proven, and must not be said:
  - (a) that RIDGID's coverage is automatic or a "lifetime warranty";
  - (b) that RIDGID is the only lifetime offer in the whole industry. "Industry's Only" is Home Depot Canada's listing wording. Attribute it.
  - (c) that Powerfist has "no warranty". Its promise names no limit, and a viewer could argue it is open-ended. The script must say it states no term and does not use the word lifetime.
- Residual risk:
  - MAXIMUM 0548799P and 4994235P list "special warranty". The MAXIMUM brand page caps cordless tools at "up to 5 years", so a lifetime term there is very unlikely. It is still not confirmed at listing level. Producer: ask a Canadian Tire store or call MAXIMUM Customer Support (1-888-670-6682), or leave those two kits out of the video.

## 4. FINAL TEN BRANDS: RECOMMENDATION

**Keep the original ten:** Mastercraft, MAXIMUM, Powerfist, Ryobi, RIDGID, DeWalt, Craftsman, Milwaukee, Makita, Bosch.

Why Craftsman stays:
- It is readily sold in Canada. RONA's current flyer runs a nine-item Craftsman power-tool promotion in Toronto, Calgary and Vancouver.
- Its Canadian terms are tier a from craftsman.ca.
- It carries the strongest documented ownership beat in the set: SBD bought it from Sears Holdings on March 8, 2017, and is paying royalties through 2032, all in U.S. dollars (tl_ownership S2, S3).

Two conditions:
- (1) On air, say "sold at RONA", not "Canadian Tire" or "Home Depot".
- (2) If no rona.ca screenshot is taken before recording, quote the flyer price as "RONA's flyer, valid until October 14". The Ah comes from craftsman.ca's title.

**Fallback (only if the producer cannot show Craftsman on a Canadian shelf or page): swap in Black and Decker.**
- Canadian Tire 0547286P BDCDD120C-2, "1 year exchange warranty redeemable at any Canadian Tire store", $89.99 on sale (was $134.99), 2 × 1.5 Ah, $30.00/Ah, at store 150.
- Its rank and recap changes are already written in tl_retention §7.3.

---

## 5. FINAL TITLE: RECOMMENDATION

- Current: "We Investigated 10 Tool Brands Sold in Canada (Only 1 Covers Your Cordless Drill for Life)" (90 characters).
- Problem: "Covers… for Life" asserts unconditional coverage. RIDGID's own terms make it conditional: registration within 90 days, receipt upload, "a promotional program", and reconditioned tools excluded. A viewer who skipped registration is not covered for life.
- **Recommended:** "We Investigated 10 Tool Brands Sold in Canada (Only 1 Offers Lifetime Coverage on Cordless Drills)" (97 characters, under YouTube's 100). "Offers" is accurate: the coverage has to be taken up.
- Alternate if the producer wants "your": "We Investigated 10 Tool Brands Sold in Canada (Only 1 Will Cover Your Cordless Drill for Life)" (94 characters). It is weaker on conditionality, so the script must state the 90-day registration within 15 seconds of the #1 reveal.
- Thumbnail "ONLY 1 FOR LIFE": keep it. It is a count and a phrase RIDGID itself uses ("FOR LIFE").
